The process executing a local ledger transition must itself remain in that exact proof-bound delegated parent cgroup. Any controller, stage, helper, Gunicorn master/worker, or other process barrier-moved into a journal-bound descendant kill domain receives no ledger/lock/proof path or capability and may request only a closed hash/identity-bound ledger RPC from its still-outside-parent supervisor; that supervisor revalidates the caller PID/start/cgroup membership and journal state, performs the ledger transition itself, and returns only the typed result/hash. A descendant opening the ledger directly or a supervisor that has left the proof-bound parent fails closed. Changed-boot recovery instead requires the immutable prior-boot proof/root binding, a fresh current-boot cgroup-v2 observation, and the exact stale-boot/F3 disappearance contracts; it never rewrites/deletes a proof or treats the new parent inode as authority for the prior domain, and only the closed resume branch may create the distinct current-boot proof through the special publisher below. The ordinary embedded/committed publication path still requires reject/close/archive to leave the canonical ledger absent before it publishes the current boot's distinct proof; `snapshot --after-archive` requires that proof, retains every prior proof, and binds only the new path/hash/identity into the next root. The sole exception is the committed `--stale-boot-resume` form at an authenticated resume-goal `claims-pruned` outer journal: it creates the new proof under the stable lock before the atomic same-attempt boot/proof rebind, retains the prior proof, and cannot run on the reject branch or with any receipt/controller/terminal decision. Tracked-CI roots require `execution_host_preflight=null`, forbid F3/F4 execution, and use no cgroup authority. Every transition and `snapshot --after-archive` must nofollow-open `lock_path`, compare this exact object before and after `flock`, and reject replacement, relink, owner/mode drift, or archived/current identity mismatch. `baseline` is exactly `{omo_lstat,evidence_lstat,shared_evidence_manifest,containers,volumes,networks,listeners}` for the creation boot; `reboot_stable_baseline_sha256` is SHA-256 over RFC-8785 canonical JSON whose arrays use the ordering below and whose projection omits only container `state|health|restart_count` and listener `socket_inode|pid|process_start_ticks` while retaining container `id|labels|config_sha256|published_ports`, complete named-volume/network entries, and listener `transport|host|port|owner_kind|container_id|executable_realpath|argv_sha256`; `boot_observation` is exactly `{boot_id,observed_at_utc,containers,volumes,networks,listeners}` using the same closed entries and the volatile fields for the current boot; `omo_lstat` is the feature-worktree binding `{path,type,mode,uid,gid,device,inode,link_target,realpath,created_by_attempt}` with `type=symlink-to-directory`, its path/link/realpath equal `authority_binding.worktree_omo_*`, `evidence_lstat` is `{path,type,mode,uid,gid,device,inode,realpath,created_by_attempt}` with `type=directory`, and the shared manifest reference is `{path,sha256,entry_count}`. Container entries are `{id,labels,config_sha256,state,health,restart_count,published_ports}`, where `config_sha256` hashes RFC-8785 canonical `{image_id,config_user,network_mode,mount_targets,published_ports,labels}` derived from Docker inspect with no command or environment values; `mount_targets` is a sorted unique absolute-string array, `published_ports` sorts by `(transport,host,port,container_port)`, and `labels` sorts by `(name,value)`; volumes `{volume_name,driver,scope,created_at,mountpoint,labels,options}`, networks `{id,name,labels}`, and every listener is `{transport,host,port,socket_inode,owner_kind,container_id,pid,process_start_ticks,executable_realpath,argv_sha256}`: container-owned listeners require only `container_id` among ownership fields and have `argv_sha256=null`, while process-owned listeners require PID/start/executable plus SHA-256 of raw NUL-delimited `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` and have `container_id=null`. Containers sort by `id`, volumes by `volume_name`, networks by `(name,id)`, and listeners by `(transport,host,port,owner_kind,container_id|null,executable_realpath|null,argv_sha256|null)`. No prose alias is a JSON key: “launcher” means `desired.launcher_path` plus `desired.launcher_lstat`, “resolved interpreter/executable” means `desired.interpreter_realpath` plus `desired.interpreter_sha256`, “argv hash” means `desired.argv_sha256`, and a member interpreter means `observed.members[].executable_realpath`.
- Normative schema-v2 claims and CLI: every claim is exactly `{claim_id,kind,status,purpose,dependency_claim_ids,root_relative_path,reserved_at_utc,prepared_at_utc,activated_at_utc,last_verified_at_utc,runner_creation,candidate_envelope_binding,desired,observed}`; `root_relative_path` is `claims/<claim_id>`. The closed claim/runner-state matrix is: every non-runner has `runner_creation=null`, uses only its existing `reserved|active` matrix, and can never be `prepared`. A browser runner permits exactly `(reserved,null|intent|remove-intent|removed)`, `(prepared,prepared|remove-intent|removed)`, and `(active,prepared|remove-intent|removed)`. `reserved` requires nonnull `reserved_at_utc`, null `prepared_at_utc|activated_at_utc`, and empty observed identities. `prepared` requires nonnull ordered `reserved_at_utc < prepared_at_utc`, null `activated_at_utc`, and the complete created-state observations. `active` requires nonnull ordered `reserved_at_utc < prepared_at_utc < activated_at_utc` and the complete active observations. For `prepared|active`, runner state `prepared -> remove-intent -> removed` retains the claim status, every lifecycle timestamp, and the complete observed object byte-for-byte; those observations become immutable cleanup-authority tombstones, not assertions of current liveness. Reserved cleanup retains the empty observed object. Across cleanup, `claim_id|kind|purpose|dependency_claim_ids|root_relative_path|desired|reserved_at_utc` and every nonnull `prepared_at_utc|activated_at_utc` are immutable; only `last_verified_at_utc` may advance monotonically and only the runner-creation removal fields may be appended. No transition demotes status, clears observations, returns from cleanup to creation/activation, or returns to an earlier runner state. A runner is operational and eligible to satisfy a dependency only at `status=active` with `runner_creation.state="prepared"`; `remove-intent|removed` is cleanup-only, cannot activate, refresh product use, accept non-bootstrap frames, or acquire a new dependent, and remains an unreleased reverse-dependent borrower of its owners until release. `last_verified_at_utc` is nonnull at every status and no timestamp may move backward. `candidate_envelope_binding` is null for every noncandidate claim and initially null for an exact `candidate-application-publisher|candidate-browser-runner-publisher`. Its sole nonnull form is exact `{schema_version:1,envelope,envelope_sha256}`, where `envelope` has the closed candidate-envelope shape below and `envelope_sha256=SHA256(RFC8785(envelope)+LF)`. Only same-boot `candidate-publish` may advance null to nonnull, once, while the candidate claim is active, both authorizations are unpublished/empty, both destinations are absent, and the staged path is the nofollow regular mode-0400 executor-owned link-count-one `<claim_root>/candidate-envelope.json`. Under the stable lock it validates the staged canonical bytes against claim/attempt/purpose/authorization/path identity, clean current SHA/tree, a fresh immutable-image inspect plus embedded manifest/labels/image contract/suite set, and requires `published_at_utc` to be no earlier than `activated_at_utc` and no later than the current six-fraction UTC timestamp captured under that stable lock; it then atomically file-and-parent-fsyncs the complete binding into the canonical ledger before any destination temp, link, or directory fsync. A nonnull binding and its timestamp/image/contract/hash are immutable, participate in the complete prior-claim hash, and are the sole source from which envelope bytes and the would-be published authorization entry are reconstructed; a supplied staged file on replay must be identical, while exact absence after binding is recoverable from the binding itself. Binding-null plus any envelope destination is corruption; binding-nonnull commits completion of both authorizations and forbids abort. A stack `desired` is exactly `{project,services,owned_volumes,owned_networks,database_names,loopback_ports,borrowed_volume_refs,borrowed_network_refs}`. Every `labels` and Docker `options` array anywhere in the root, baseline, desired, or observed schema is a sorted unique array of exact string entries `{name,value}`; absent/null Engine maps normalize to `[]`, never to an alternate shape. All arrays are lexicographically sorted and unique by their complete scalar value or, for objects, by the identity tuple stated here: services by `name`, owned volumes by `volume_name`, owned networks by `network_name`, database names by value, ports by `(transport,host,port)`, borrowed resources by `(owner_claim_id,resource_name)`, observed containers by ID, and observed resources by their corresponding name. Each `owned_volumes[]` item is exactly `{volume_name,driver,labels}`, with `driver="local"` and `labels` a sorted unique array of exact `{name,value}` strings containing the required attempt/claim/resource labels; each `owned_networks[]` item is exactly `{network_name,driver,internal,attachable,labels}`, with `driver="bridge"`, boolean `internal`/`attachable`, and the same closed label-entry shape. `database_names[]` contains only strings matching `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,62}$`; each `loopback_ports[]` item is exactly `{transport,host,port}`, with `transport="tcp"`, `host="127.0.0.1"|"::1"`, integer port 1..65535, and port 5432 forbidden. Each desired service is exactly `{name,image_id,uid,gid,command,start_policy,network_mode,network_refs,extra_hosts,volume_mounts,published_ports,environment_contract,image_contract,filesystem_contract}`; `start_policy=running-before-activation|prepared-attest-before-use`, `network_mode=bridge|host|none`, each network ref is exactly `{network_name,aliases}`, each extra host `{hostname,address}`, each volume mount `{volume_name,target,read_only}`, each published port `{transport,host,port,container_port}`, and each service `environment_contract` exactly `{literal,secret_keys,absent_keys}`, where `literal` is a sorted array of exact nonsecret `{name,value}` entries and both key-name arrays are sorted, unique, and disjoint. `image_contract` is null only for immutable third-party images. Every project-authored application or browser-runner service uses exactly `{kind,revision_sha,tree_sha,source_manifest_sha256,source_entry_count,available_suite_ids}`: `kind="application"|"browser-runner"`; SHAs are lowercase fixed-width hex; the count is a nonnegative integer; an application has `available_suite_ids=[]`; and a browser runner has a sorted unique subset of `["availability","patient","runtime-https","scheduling"]` derived from its embedded manifest. `filesystem_contract` is null for non-runner services and for every runner is exactly `{root_read_only,tmpfs_mounts,ipc_mode,shm_size_bytes,writable_paths}`; each tmpfs item is exactly `{target,size_bytes,mode,uid,gid,nosuid,nodev,noexec}`, `ipc_mode="private"|"none"`, `shm_size_bytes` is a nonnegative integer, and `writable_paths` is a sorted unique absolute application-data allowlist. For ordinary services activation verifies Docker inspect, exact numeric `Config.User=<uid>:<gid>`, project-authored image labels and embedded manifest, `ReadonlyRootfs`, `Tmpfs`, `IpcMode`, `ShmSize`, container mountinfo, and causal write probes without persisting secret values. Runner `runner-create` first durably records its creation intent, then verifies only created-state inspectable config and atomically records `prepared_at_utc`, the exact container identity, and `runner_creation.state="prepared"`; its later bootstrap attestation supplies mountinfo/runtime PID and causal probes, and only the atomic `activate` transition records those observations and permits non-bootstrap frames. Bridge services require nonempty network refs; host/none services require none; every service reference resolves to exactly one owned or borrowed resource. Stack `observed` is exactly `{services,container_ids,owned_volumes,borrowed_volumes,owned_networks,borrowed_networks,listeners}`. `container_ids[]` contains sorted unique lowercase 64-hex Docker container IDs. Each `observed.owned_volumes[]` item is exactly `{volume_name,driver,scope,created_at,mountpoint,labels,options}` and each borrowed item is exactly `{owner_claim_id,volume_name,driver,scope,created_at,mountpoint,labels,options,access}`, where `access="read-only"`, `scope="local"`, `created_at` is Docker Engine RFC3339, `mountpoint` is its absolute inspect value, and `labels`/`options` are sorted unique exact `{name,value}` arrays. Docker named volumes intentionally have no invented immutable ID: their identity is the full fresh-inspect tuple `(volume_name,driver,scope,created_at,mountpoint,labels,options)`; activation and every refresh byte-compare that tuple, and a missing/recreated/drifted volume fails. A borrowed tuple must equal the active owner tuple exactly. Each `observed.owned_networks[]` item is exactly `{network_name,network_id,driver,internal,attachable,labels}` and each borrowed item is exactly `{owner_claim_id,network_name,network_id,driver,internal,attachable,labels,access}`, where `network_id` is lowercase 64-hex, `access="attach"`, and every borrowed tuple equals its active owner tuple. Every observed service is exactly `{name,container_id,image_id,uid,gid,command,state,network_mode,network_attachments,extra_hosts,volume_mounts,published_ports,environment_contract,image_contract,filesystem_contract}`; each network attachment is exactly `{network_name,network_id,aliases}`; `state="created"|"running"`, with `created` legal only while the runner claim is `prepared` and `running` mandatory before that claim becomes `active`. Every listener is exactly `{transport,host,port,socket_inode,owner_kind,container_id,pid,process_start_ticks,executable_realpath,argv_sha256}` with the closed nullable ownership fields and process/container matrix defined by the root schema. Desired borrowed volume refs are exactly `{owner_claim_id,volume_name,access}` with `access="read-only"` and desired borrowed network refs exactly `{owner_claim_id,network_name,access}` with `access="attach"`. A filesystem `desired` is exactly `{owned_files,published_outputs}`: owned-file entries are `{relative_path,mode,uid,gid,sha256}` beneath the claim root, while each published-output authorization is exactly `{authorization_id,output_kind,root_path,governing_lock,predecessor_authorization_ids,relative_paths,mode,uid,gid}` with a claim-unique lowercase-hyphenated `authorization_id`, `output_kind="todo-evidence"|"candidate-image"|"publication-history"|"final-terminal"`, `governing_lock="stable"|"final"`, a sorted unique predecessor-ID array referring only to earlier authorizations in the same claim, a nonempty fixed sorted relative-path allowlist, and a canonical absolute root constrained respectively to the current attempt's `todo-evidence/`, its `candidate-images/`, its `publication-history/`, or the frozen final control root's exact `terminal/` directory. Candidate-image and publication-history authorizations always use the stable lock. A filesystem claim whose purpose is `candidate-application-publisher` has exactly `candidate-application-envelope -> candidate_image_root:[<sha>/application-envelope.json]` with no predecessor and `candidate-application-publication-history -> publication_history_root:[<claim_id>.json]` after the envelope; a claim whose purpose is `candidate-browser-runner-publisher` has exactly `candidate-browser-runner-envelope -> candidate_image_root:[<sha>/browser-runner-envelope.json]` with no predecessor and `candidate-browser-runner-publication-history -> publication_history_root:[<claim_id>.json]` after the envelope. Each of these four authorizations is a one-file mode-0400 executor-UID/GID allowlist and neither candidate purpose permits another authorization or an adjacent receipt. Final-terminal capability is split into these exact atomic authorization IDs and allowlists: `input-launcher-path -> [F3-launcher.path]`; `input-launcher-lstat -> [F3-launcher.lstat]` after `input-launcher-path`; `input-launcher-sha256 -> [F3-launcher.sha256]` after `input-launcher-lstat`; `input-manifest -> [inputs.json]` after all three launcher authorizations; `input-sidecar -> [inputs.sha256]` after `input-manifest`; `f1-verdict -> [F1-verdict.json]`; `f1-receipt -> [F1-receipt.txt]` after `f1-verdict`; `f2-prerequisites -> [F2-prerequisites.json]`; `f2-verdict -> [F2-verdict.json]` after `f2-prerequisites`; `f2-receipt -> [F2-receipt.txt]` after `f2-verdict`; `f3-artifacts` contains only every exact synthetic PNG/JSON relative path from the frozen `runtime-https` suite allowlist stored as individual literals in `inputs.json`; `f3-receipt -> [F3/receipt.json]` after `f3-artifacts`; `f3-manifest -> [F3/manifest.json]` after `f3-receipt`; `f4-pre -> [F4-pre.txt]`; and `f4-final -> [F4-final.txt]` after `f4-pre`. No authorization may contain another phase, suite, lane, or parent path. `observed` is exactly `{owned_files,published_outputs}`; owned files add nonnegative `device`/`inode`. `observed.published_outputs` has exactly one item per desired authorization, sorted by `authorization_id`, and every item is exactly `{authorization_id,output_kind,root_path,governing_lock,status,entries}`. Status is per authorization, so a claim may legally contain a mixture of completed and pending phases. Before that authorization's publication `status="unpublished"` and `entries=[]`; publication is legal only when every predecessor is already `published` and byte-unchanged. After one locked no-replace transaction `status="published"` and `entries` is a sorted exact array of `{relative_path,mode,uid,gid,size_bytes,sha256}` whose relative paths equal that authorization's complete desired allowlist with no missing/extra entry; no partial state exists within an authorization. The multi-file `f3-artifacts` authorization publishes its complete runtime-HTTPS directory by one no-replace directory rename before `artifact-published`, while receipt and manifest use their later distinct authorizations. Mutable staging always remains below the claim root; publishing fsyncs and transfers authority to the immutable todo/final journal. Claim release requires staging absent, every published authorization byte-unchanged, and every still-unpublished authorization empty; lane success separately requires its exact success authorization set fully published. A process `desired` is exactly `{process_model,launcher_path,launcher_lstat,interpreter_realpath,interpreter_sha256,argv,argv_sha256,module,worker_count,gunicorn_config_path,gunicorn_config_lstat,gunicorn_config_sha256,uid,gid,environment_contract,host_ports,borrowed_file_refs}`; the three Gunicorn-config fields are all nonnull only for `process_model=gunicorn-prefork`, identify the clean tracked `ops/container/gunicorn_no_proxy.py` file, and are all null for `single`; `launcher_path` is absolute, `launcher_lstat` is exactly `{device,inode,mode,uid,gid,link_count,symlink_target}`, and `interpreter_realpath`/`interpreter_sha256` identify the executable reached by that launcher; its environment contract has the same closed shape and no secret value. Process `observed` is exactly `{members,listener_socket_inode,listeners}`; every member is exactly `{pid,ppid,pgid,sid,start_ticks,uid,gid,executable_realpath,argv_sha256}`. Every `borrowed_file_refs[]` entry is exactly `{owner_claim_id,relative_path,mode,uid,gid,device,inode,sha256,access}`, with `access="read-only"`, nonnegative device/inode copied from the active owner, and no extra key. The only CLI grammar is one of local `snapshot --approved-plan <absolute-file> --authority-workspace <absolute-dir> --authority-root <absolute-dir> --foundation-sha <40-hex> --worktree <absolute-dir> --execution-host-preflight <absolute-mode-0400-json>` or hosted `snapshot --approved-plan <absolute-file> --tracked-ci-sidecar <file> --foundation-sha <40-hex> --worktree <absolute-dir>`, `verify [--refresh] [--claim <uuid>]`, `claim --spec <mode-0400-json>`, candidate-envelope bind/publication `candidate-publish --claim <uuid> --staged-envelope <absolute-path>`, runner-only `runner-create --claim <uuid>`, same-boot runner-intent cleanup `runner-discard --claim <uuid> --reason owner-cleanup|owner-lost|unsafe-state`, `activate --claim <uuid> --observed <mode-0400-json>`, one of same-boot `reconcile`, changed-boot nonsealed-cleanup `reconcile --stale-boot`, or sealed acceptance/USER-gate `reconcile --terminal-final <absolute-mode-0400-final.json> --sha <40-hex>` (which on success prints exactly the nofollow-validated absolute current-boot terminal-revalidation artifact path plus LF), `release --claim <uuid>`, `authorize-user-fix --sha <40-hex> --terminal-revalidation <absolute-mode-0400-json> --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final`, one of read-only `rejection-context --sha <40-hex> --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final --format nul` or read-only explicit-user `rejection-context --user-requested-fix --user-authorization <absolute-mode-0400-json> --sha <40-hex> --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final --format nul`, `reject [--user-authorization <absolute-mode-0400-current-binding.json>] --sha <40-hex> --reason <lane>:<class> [--reason <lane>:<class> ...] --inputs-sha256 <64hex|none> --pre-f4-sha256 <64hex|none> --final-sha256 <64hex|none>`, `close [--user-authorization <absolute-mode-0400-current-binding.json>] [--terminal-revalidation <absolute-mode-0400-json>] [--final-receipt <path>]`, where during open-ledger accepted-close preparation `--terminal-revalidation` is forbidden on the creation boot and mandatory after changed-boot terminal reconciliation, while exact closed-ledger receipt-only recovery may omit it and, if supplied, requires the journal's immutable historical binding; `--final-receipt` is present only for accepted success, `archive-rollover --closed-attempt <uuid> --rejected-sha <40-hex> --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final --rejection-spec-sha256 <64-hex>`, and retry/recovery `snapshot --after-archive <uuid> --approved-plan <absolute-file> --authority-workspace <absolute-dir> --authority-root <absolute-dir> --foundation-sha <40-hex> --worktree <absolute-dir> --execution-host-preflight <absolute-mode-0400-json>`; The ordinary `rejection-context` form derives reasons only from the complete stable-lock failure-receipt set and rejects an empty set. `authorize-user-fix` separates durable user intent from boot-scoped observation. On its first user-triggered invocation it no-replace creates-or-byte-validates and file/parent-fsyncs mode-0400 `<attempt_root>/user-fix-intent.json` with deterministic exact keys `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,sha,tree_sha,final_sha256,approvals_sha256,authorized_reason:"USER:user-requested-fix"}`; intent bytes contain no boot ID, terminal path/hash, or timestamp. An exact existing intent is recovery authority for the same request without another user prompt; mismatch, failure receipts, different SHA/tree/FINAL/approval digest, accepted close, or non-USER rejection fails closed. After validating the current-boot terminal-revalidation artifact, requiring the current open-ledger SHA-256 to equal its `post_update_ledger_sha256`, requiring `claims=[]` and zero task resources, and requiring publisher `released` with journal SHA-256 equal to `publisher_final_journal_sha256`, the command no-replace creates-or-nofollow-validates executor-owned mode-0700 `<attempt_root>/user-fix-bindings/` with parent fsync, then create-or-byte-validates and file/parent-fsyncs mode-0400 `<attempt_root>/user-fix-bindings/<current-boot-id>.json` with exact keys `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,sha,current_boot_id,boot_changed,user_fix_intent_relative_path,user_fix_intent_sha256,terminal_revalidation_relative_path,terminal_revalidation_sha256,approvals_sha256}`. Its paths are exactly `user-fix-intent.json` and `terminal-revalidation/<current-boot-id>.json`; hashes and `boot_changed` equal those artifacts. Prior-boot bindings remain immutable and never select current authority. Same-boot retry byte-validates and reuses the existing binding; a later boot appends exactly one binding after terminal reconciliation. The command prints only the nofollow-validated absolute current binding path plus LF, so crashes after either publication/fsync but before stdout resume idempotently. The explicit `rejection-context --user-requested-fix --user-authorization` form accepts only that current-boot binding, independently validates its fixed intent and current terminal artifact, requires the current ledger to remain byte-for-byte at the artifact’s `post_update_ledger_sha256` with zero claims/resources and the matching released publisher journal, is legal before rejection-spec creation or with the already-existing exact USER-only spec, emits only `USER:user-requested-fix`, and cannot combine with a failure reason. `rejection-context` stable-lock validates the open ledger, receipt or USER intent/binding aggregate, phase journal, authority binding, approved plan, worktree, and current terminal artifact, then emits exactly nine NUL-terminated fixed fields `(attempt_id,inputs_sha256|none,pre_f4_sha256|none,final_sha256|none,authority_workspace,authority_root,approved_plan,worktree,terminal_revalidation_path|none)` followed by one or more sorted unique `<lane>:<class>` reasons. Fixed field 8 is close-only: ordinary rejection emits `none`; USER emits `none` only when the supplied current binding and terminal artifact both say `boot_changed=false` and their boot equals attempt creation, otherwise it emits that binding's authenticated absolute current terminal path. A prior-boot binding always rejects, even if once valid; field 8 is never derived from the boot-stable intent or an earlier binding. Every path is absolute/control-character-free and every USER context or mutating command independently revalidates the exact current binding and intent plus the unchanged artifact post-update ledger hash; a pre-update or later-refreshed ledger never authenticates. `runner_creation` is exact object-or-null `{schema_version,state,creation_boot_id,intent_id,intent_at_utc,service_name,container_name,desired_service_sha256,create_argv_sha256,intent_sha256,container_id,remove_reason,remove_intent_at_utc,removed_at_utc}`. In a canonical claim `remove_reason` is exactly `owner-cleanup|owner-lost|unsafe-state` when nonnull; `changed-boot` is forbidden. In an outer `runner_recoveries[]` copy, stale reconciliation sets `remove_reason="changed-boot"` only when advancing copied canonical `intent|prepared` to copied `remove-intent`, while a copy that already held canonical `remove-intent` preserves its existing same-boot reason, container ID, and timestamp byte-for-byte; stale recovery never rewrites that reason. Its immutable `intent_sha256` hashes canonical `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,claim_id,creation_boot_id,intent_id,service_name,container_name,desired_service_sha256,create_argv_sha256}`; the sole service name comes from the immutable runner desired spec, `container_name="clinic-phase1a-runner-"+claim_id`, and Docker create carries the exact attempt/claim/service labels plus `clinic.phase1a.runner-create-intent=<intent_sha256>`. In `intent`, the container/removal fields are null. In `prepared`, `container_id` is the sole observed created-container ID and removal fields are null. In `remove-intent`, `remove_reason|remove_intent_at_utc` are nonnull and every previously nonnull identity field and timestamp is unchanged. A previously recorded `container_id` is immutable; intent-only cleanup may set it once to the sole exact union-matched ID before deletion, or retain null only after authenticated exact absence when no ID was ever durably captured. In `removed`, every preceding field, including nullable or nonnull `container_id`, is preserved and only `removed_at_utc` becomes nonnull. Removal timestamps obey the global non-backward rule, and `last_verified_at_utc` is no earlier than every nonnull event timestamp. `runner-create` is the sole real create/prepare path: under the stable lock it atomically fsyncs `reserved/null -> reserved/intent` before Docker create, derives the exact argv rather than accepting it, and enumerates the union of exact deterministic-name and intent-label matches. Zero candidates on the creation boot with the still-live journal-authenticated owning controller retries that identical create; exactly one candidate may be adopted only by that same live owner on that boot after exact name/labels/config/image/user/command/environment/mount/network/rootfs equality, state `created`, PID zero, and restart count zero prove it never started. On the creation boot, cancellation routes through `runner-discard --reason owner-cleanup`, a same-boot dead/recovering owner routes through `runner-discard --reason owner-lost`, and an exact identity/configuration-matched prematurely started intended container routes through `runner-discard --reason unsafe-state`; none is adopted, and the unsafe-state path removes then fails. After any boot-ID change, `runner-create` and `runner-discard` both fail before adoption, Docker stop/remove, or canonical mutation; only `reconcile --stale-boot` may clean the runner through its outer `runner_recoveries` entry. Selector disagreement, a missing/changed intent label, a foreign name collision, multiple candidates, or any identity drift blocks without deletion. After create/adoption, one atomic replace records the complete created observation, `prepared_at_utc`, claim `status="prepared"`, and runner-creation `prepared`. `runner-discard` is the sole same-boot direct physical cleanup authority for a runner with nonnull creation state; after parsing one of the three canonical reasons and while retaining the stable lock, it proves before any Docker action or write that `current_boot_id == ledger.boot_id == runner_creation.creation_boot_id`. Same-boot `reconcile` may delegate to it; `reconcile --stale-boot` never may. It accepts only `reserved/intent`, `prepared/prepared`, `active/prepared`, or an idempotent replay of the same origin status at `remove-intent|removed`; revalidates the DAG and zero reverse dependents; preserves the originating claim status, timestamps, desired data, and observations while atomically advancing the canonical claim through `remove-intent -> removed`; removes only the exact authorized container ID or authenticates exact absence; then removes the mutable claim root and releases only from `removed`. A prepared or active ID is authorized only by unchanged `container_id` plus matching name, intent label, and immutable configuration; intent-only cleanup uses the exact union-selection rules. Multiple, foreign, recreated, selector-disagreeing, or drifted candidates block without deletion. Same-boot direct release is legal only from `reserved/null` after exact absence or from canonical runner state `removed`; direct release from `intent|prepared|remove-intent` is forbidden even when the container is absent. `reconcile --stale-boot` never invokes that ledger-mutating form. Instead, its outer journal contains one exact `runner_recoveries` entry for each runner-container action, binds the complete prior claim by hash, and advances only the copied `runner_creation` through the identical removal-field matrix. It file-and-parent-fsyncs copied `remove-intent` before the first Docker stop/remove call, removes only the exact authorized ID or accepts authenticated exact absence, proves the deterministic-name/intent-label union absent, then atomically fsyncs copied `removed` together with completion of that resource action. Throughout changed-boot physical cleanup the canonical prior claim remains byte-identical and supplies the complete observed cleanup-authority tombstone; it is pruned only after every runner entry is `removed` and all resources are absent. The journal-bound prior DAG preserves each dependency owner through borrower cleanup, and changed-boot recovery never adopts. The committed publisher has only `ops/testing/execution_host_preflight.py publish --authority-root <absolute-dir>` for the ordinary ledger-absent/post-archive case or `ops/testing/execution_host_preflight.py publish --authority-root <absolute-dir> --stale-boot-resume <absolute-mode-0600-journal> --stable-lock-fd <nonnegative-int>` for the sole journal-gated resume exception. Both run the byte-equivalent embedded algorithm, serialize on the stable lock when it exists, reject either archive marker, create-or-byte-validate only the current-boot proof, retain every prior proof, and print exactly its absolute path plus LF. The special form is spawned by the outer reconciler with `pass_fds` while that reconciler holds the stable flock; it fstat/path-validates the inherited open-file-description fd and nonblocking-reasserts the same lock before it additionally nofollow-validates the open ledger plus exact resume-goal `claims-pruned` stale journal, zero task resources/receipts/controller recoveries, current boot/parent/baseline observations, and no terminal decision before permitting creation with the ledger present. unknown flags, positional arguments, aliases, combinations, or omitted required fields fail before mutation.
- Candidate-image envelope and publication-history contract: candidate metadata is never written as unclaimed attempt-root data. Each application assembler reserves and activates one canonical-UUID filesystem claim with exact purpose `candidate-application-publisher`; each browser-runner assembler does the same with purpose `candidate-browser-runner-publisher`, both before build-context creation. The application claim has exactly the two stable-lock authorizations `candidate-application-envelope -> candidate_image_root:[<sha>/application-envelope.json]` with no predecessor and `candidate-application-publication-history -> publication_history_root:[<claim_id>.json]` after that envelope. The runner claim has exactly `candidate-browser-runner-envelope -> candidate_image_root:[<sha>/browser-runner-envelope.json]` with no predecessor and `candidate-browser-runner-publication-history -> publication_history_root:[<claim_id>.json]` after that envelope. Each authorization has one file, published under the stable lock by same-directory mode-0400 temp creation, file `fsync`, no-replace `linkat`, destination-directory `fsync`, temp unlink, and second directory `fsync`; an existing destination is accepted only by byte-identical authorization replay. Each canonical envelope has exact keys `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,claim_id,authorization_id,revision_sha,tree_sha,image_id,image_contract,published_at_utc}` and embeds the complete closed application or browser-runner image contract; there is no adjacent receipt and therefore no multi-file or partial-pair state. `candidate-publish --claim <uuid> --staged-envelope <absolute-path>` accepts only `<claim_root>/candidate-envelope.json`; callers supply no destination, authorization ID, image kind, history path, timestamp, contract override, or mutable tag, and only the two committed image controllers may invoke it. On the first call it performs the normative fresh-inspect checks above and atomically binds the complete post-build canonical envelope bytes/hash into the active claim before any destination write; after that durable binding, the command derives kind, envelope authorization/root/path, exact bytes, and would-be observed entry solely from the bound claim and publishes only the envelope. Same-boot crash replay may reconstruct a missing staged file from the binding, but can never regenerate a timestamp, image ID, contract, SHA/tree, or authorization. The sole candidate-specific branch of `release --claim <uuid>` first requires context/staging absence and a byte-valid published envelope, then generates and publishes through the second authorization the immutable mode-0400 `<publication_history_root>/<claim_id>.json` whose closed canonical keys are `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,claim_id,purpose,authorization_id,output_kind,root_path,relative_path,predecessor_authorization_id,predecessor_root_path,predecessor_entries_sha256,candidate_envelope_binding_sha256}`. Exact values are schema version 1, the claim's exact purpose, the matching `candidate-*-publication-history` authorization ID, `output_kind="publication-history"`, `root_path=<publication_history_root>`, `relative_path="<claim_id>.json"`, the matching envelope predecessor ID/root, SHA-256 of RFC-8785 canonical predecessor `observed.entries` or the stale action's byte-identical would-be entries, and `candidate_envelope_binding_sha256=SHA256(RFC8785(candidate_envelope_binding))`; the record has no release timestamp or self-hash. Todo 1's ledger code is the sole history-byte generator and publisher. The legal ordinary state sequence is exactly `(binding=null,envelope=unpublished,history=unpublished) -> (binding=nonnull,unpublished,unpublished) -> (nonnull,published,unpublished) -> (nonnull,published,published) -> claim absent`; history-first, publication while binding-null, and binding mutation are impossible. History publication is file+parent-fsynced, then its observed authorization is atomically recorded, and only after both authorizations revalidate and staging is absent may the ledger remove the claim. A crash with the exact envelope destination present while its authorization is still ledger-unpublished, or the exact history destination present while history is ledger-unpublished, is a replay-only prefix: same-boot code byte-validates against the binding, advances only that authorization, and never regenerates bytes; claim absence proves completed release. Before binding, abort may release only when both destinations and staging are absent; after binding, abort is forbidden even when no destination exists. Once the envelope exists, generic/direct release cannot bypass history. Candidate envelopes and publication-history tombstones survive release. Same-boot `reconcile` uses the identical bound-envelope publication/release routine and refuses any binding or staged-byte drift. Changed-boot `reconcile --stale-boot` leaves the canonical prior claim byte-identical and, for every bound candidate whose canonical history is unpublished, freezes three unconditional ordered outer resource actions, including the fixed idempotent staging action even when its path is already absent. `publish-or-adopt-candidate-envelope` is bound to the complete prior-claim hash, binding hash, envelope authorization/root/path, and exact would-be published entry; it validates an already canonical-published entry or, when canonical envelope state is unpublished, reconstructs from the binding and no-replace publishes or byte-validates an already linked exact envelope before completing only the outer action. After that action, the unconditional `candidate-staging-complete-<claim_id>` / `filesystem-staging -> remove-staging` action nofollow-validates and unlinks the exact binding-derived staged file when present or authenticates its exact absence when already gone, file-and-parent-fsyncs any removal, and completes only its outer action; only then may `publish-or-adopt-candidate-history` derive the exact timestamp-free record and predecessor digest from the same would-be entry, no-replace publish or byte-validate history, and complete only its outer action. None of the three outer actions synthesizes an intermediate canonical authorization state; claims-prune is the sole canonical ledger replacement. A binding-null candidate may be pruned only after both destinations and staging are absent. A bound candidate may be pruned only with staging absent and either both canonical authorizations already published and revalidated or the exact ordered envelope, staging, and history outer actions completed with both destinations byte-valid. The final input freezer requires the two exact SHA-specific envelopes plus their two exact claim-ID tombstones, reconstructs each binding from its envelope, validates the history `candidate_envelope_binding_sha256`, validates envelope image fields against the inspected immutable images plus the exact purpose/authorization/root/predecessor/entries digest, proves both claim UUIDs absent from the current ledger, and binds all four paths/hashes into `inputs.json`. Crash recovery can finish the binding, publish or adopt one exact bound atomic envelope, then publish or adopt its separately authorized tombstone, or reject an extra/replaced/unbound file. Binding-nonnull with envelope absent, envelope present but ledger-unpublished, envelope recorded with history absent, and history linked but unrecorded are recoverable only with the exact live claim or its authenticated ordered three-action stale sequence; binding-null plus envelope, claim-absent envelope-only state, and post-release binding or tombstone reconstruction are forbidden. Concurrent identical replay converges to one pair; different bytes, IDs, roots, purposes, predecessors, or hashes fail unchanged. No adjacent receipt, legacy pair, multi-file candidate authorization, history-first state, generic-release bypass, or partial state within either one-file authorization is legal.

- Persistent final-terminal publisher: all final-terminal authorizations belong to exactly one filesystem claim whose `purpose="final-terminal-publisher"` and whose `claim_id` is a newly allocated canonical lowercase UUID, never a role name. Before any publisher claim root, staging file, terminal output, or ledger reservation exists, the input controller holds stable-lock-then-final-lock, validates open ledger bytes `L0` with no publisher, allocates one UUID and canonical reservation timestamp, constructs the complete schema-fixed reservation spec `S` and prospective ledger bytes `L1`, and atomically file+parent-fsyncs `terminal_publisher_state=id-bound` with that UUID plus immutable `terminal_publisher_reservation_at_utc`, `terminal_publisher_reservation_spec_sha256=SHA256(RFC8785(S))`, `terminal_publisher_pre_reservation_ledger_sha256=SHA256(L0)`, and `terminal_publisher_post_reservation_ledger_sha256=SHA256(L1)`. Only then does it temp-fsync/replace/parent-fsync the canonical ledger from `L0` to `L1`, after which it atomically fsyncs journal state `reserved` without changing any reservation binding. Recovery at `id-bound` accepts only the pre-hash with publisher absent or the post-hash with the same UUID present as the exact reserved spec with empty observations/authorizations and no publisher root, staging, or output; it performs the bound ledger replacement only in the first case and then advances the journal to `reserved`. On an ordinary creation-boot or post-resume entry, activation creates-or-validates only the exact empty claim root, atomically changes that ledger claim to active, then fsyncs journal `active`; `journal=reserved,ledger=active` is the sole crash-after-activation prefix and advances after full validation, while `journal=active,ledger=reserved` blocks. F1/F2 reviewer scratch, F3 orchestration, and every lane resource claim are separate staging-only claims with no final-terminal authorization. Each successful lane stages terminal bytes only below its own claim root; its supervising controller validates those bytes and asks the one persistent publisher, while holding the prescribed stable/final lock, to publish the exact allowlist. For F4, `final_artifact_gate.sh` receives no terminal/control-root destination or publisher capability and stages only `<final-gate-claim>/staging/F4-final.txt` plus the mode-0400 typed `<final-gate-claim>/staging/F4-outcome.json`; the outside-parent controller validates that durable handoff and the persistent publisher alone creates `terminal/F4-final.txt` through the existing `f4-final` authorization. `final.json` is not a final-terminal authorization: it is the immutable control inventory created only by a separately supervised final freezer after the approving `f4-final` authorization is durable. Staging files, typed handoffs, receipts, and `final.json` are never members of `PRE_F4` or terminal `FINAL`. A failed F1, F2, F4, or ORCH lane publishes no terminal authorization: after cleanup its controller seals its failure-ready journal and writes only the canonical common receipt beneath `<failure_receipt_root>` under the stable lock, and the publisher never opens that root. A schema-valid semantic F4 `REJECT` is not a controller failure and retains only its canonical rejecting `f4-final` authorization under the separate `f4-rejected` rule. F3 may already have immutably published the predecessor-valid `f3-artifacts` authorization before a later controller/session failure; that exact partial prefix remains published, but `f3-receipt|f3-manifest` remain unpublished and its supervisor writes only the common F3 receipt. The publisher remains active and fresh across F1, F2, F3, `scope-pre`, `pre-f4`, and `final`, so every predecessor reference resolves to this immutable UUID. It never jumps directly from `active` to claim removal. After either the complete approved terminal chain exists or every failure-ready journal plus authenticated stable-lock common-receipt aggregate exists, the controller first validates the exact immutable approved chain or rejection-branch prefix—every already-successful lane authorization plus optional `f3-artifacts`, every failed/unrun/successor authorization unpublished with an empty destination—and atomically fsyncs `terminal_publisher_state=release-intent` with the complete exact release fields defined below before touching the ledger. Publication is permanently forbidden from release-intent onward. On a same-boot approved/rejected branch, the bound ledger update removes only this claim and must produce the journaled post-release ledger hash. On stale-boot rejection recovery, the outer journal retains this as the sole possible claim after all task resources and nonpublisher claims are absent, finalizes every bound F3/controller receipt first, then the publisher fsyncs its stale-boot release intent and the one bound atomic ledger replace both releases it and writes the new boot observation. A stale-boot quiescent resume preserves an exact reserved publisher without activation; its first later ordinary final-wave entry may activate it only through the exact ledger/journal crash-prefix rule. A stale-boot rejection never activates that reserved publisher and may instead bind `reserved -> release-intent` only when its claim root and staging are absent and its complete authorization vector and every destination are empty. A sealed approved-chain recovery is also legal only through `reconcile --terminal-final`: with zero nonpublisher claims/resources it revalidates FINAL and the authorization vector under stable-lock-then-final-lock order, crash-replays `active|release-intent|released` to released, and requires the exact ledger with `claims=[]`. A newly created post-reboot approved intent has context `stale-boot`, but its post-release ledger differs only by publisher removal and deliberately retains the prior boot observation/proof; a durable earlier-boot intent is immutable and replays its own exact pre/post hashes. Terminal revalidation separately journals and performs any boot-only ledger update after publisher release. Release recovery accepts only the journaled pre-release ledger hash with that exact publisher claim present, in which case it performs the one bound update, or the post-release ledger hash with the claim absent, in which case it finishes the publisher journal; any other pair for that release transition blocks. Only after claim absence is proven does it fsync `released_at_utc` and `terminal_publisher_state=released`; the UUID, intent, basis, authorization digest, and both ledger hashes remain immutable permanently. `id-bound` is the durable write-ahead reservation intent and permits only its exact pre-hash/absent or post-hash/exact-reserved mixed prefix and convergence to `reserved`; `reserved` permits either crash-replayable ordinary activation of its exact spec or the rejection-only all-empty `reserved -> release-intent` transition defined above; `active` permits only predecessor-valid publication/recovery; `release-intent` permits only the bound claim-removal replay; and `released` permits no publication or recreation. Reservation UUID/spec/timestamp/pre/post hashes remain immutable provenance through activation, stale boot/proof rebind, release, and archive. The generic `release --claim` form rejects this purpose unless routed through that journaled transition; no named, regenerated, released, or cross-claim predecessor is legal.
- Borrower-release and published-output contract: a borrower release never requires or permits deletion of an owner resource. Reconciliation validates the active owner's fresh inspect tuple, proves the borrower's own mount/attachment/FD/process identities absent, releases only the borrower, then later releases the owner in reverse dependency order. A filesystem claim owns mutable staging only beneath `claim_root`; a `published_outputs` entry is a pre-reserved immutable publication capability, not a writable arbitrary path. Todo evidence publishes under the stable lock and final-lane evidence under the exact shared `final.lock` authorization set; both use nofollow staging, fixed relative allowlists, file+directory `fsync`, and no-replace rename. Each atomic authorization publishes only after its declared predecessors, so prerequisite, verdict, artifact, receipt, and manifest phases never require a partially published authorization. After any publication the governing attempt/final journal owns the immutable bytes, the claim records their hashes only for release verification, and neither cleanup nor claim release removes them; release validates published entries unchanged and unpublished entries still empty.
- Reboot-stable and terminal-revalidation contract: initial snapshot stores the full creation-boot observations plus `reboot_stable_baseline_sha256` over the exact canonical projection above. Same-boot ordinary verification compares the full observations. A changed boot blocks every ordinary consumer and admits only changed-boot stale recovery for an open nonsealed attempt or `reconcile --terminal-final` for a sealed `final-frozen` acceptance/USER/prepared-open accepted-close gate with zero nonpublisher claims/resources; that terminal form first recovers any bound approved publisher to `released` and zero claims. At stale-recovery entry, a final-wave journal at `id-bound` is first normalized under stable-lock-then-final-lock using only its immutable reservation pre/post hashes: pre/absent performs the bound ledger reservation, post/exact-reserved performs none, and either advances the journal to `reserved`; every other pair blocks. This finishes an already-committed resource-free write-ahead transition before the outer journal is prepared, is not activation or cleanup, and makes the normalized reserved journal hash the later `publisher_initial_journal_sha256`. Before any cleanup side effect, `reconcile --stale-boot` nofollow-creates-or-validates mode-0600 `<stale_boot_recovery_state>` under the stable lock with exact canonical keys `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,previous_boot_id,current_boot_id,recovery_goal,state,prior_ledger_sha256,resource_actions,runner_recoveries,completed_action_ids,reboot_stable_baseline_sha256,boot_observation,boot_observation_sha256,shared_evidence_manifest_sha256,lock_identity_sha256,post_cleanup_ledger_sha256,post_update_ledger_sha256,resume_execution_host_preflight_path,resume_execution_host_preflight_sha256,f3_recovery_required,f3_journal_path,f3_initial_journal_sha256,f3_final_journal_sha256,f3_receipt_path,f3_receipt_sha256,controller_recoveries,publisher_claim_id,publisher_final_wave_journal_path,publisher_initial_journal_sha256,publisher_release_intent_sha256,publisher_final_journal_sha256,failure_receipts_sha256,updated_at_utc}`. `recovery_goal` is immutable `resume|reject`; `state` is exactly `prepared|cleaning|resources-absent|claims-prune-intent|claims-pruned|resume-proof-published|receipts-finalizing|receipts-finalized|publisher-release-intent|boot-updated|publisher-released|complete`. Prepared selects `resume` only when there is no common failure receipt, semantic rejection, terminal acceptance/USER intent, nonterminal or sealed-not-yet-receipted F1/F2/final-wave/F3 controller, or live controller lease; any existing final-wave journal is at an authenticated quiescent nonterminal phase with no running form. It selects `reject` exactly when one or more existing receipts or bound controller/F3 recoveries make an authenticated rejection inevitable. Ambiguous/mixed state blocks; the branch never changes. At prepared, `f3_recovery_required=true` exactly for a nonterminal or sealed-not-yet-receipted F3 journal; then its path/initial hash are nonnull and final journal/receipt fields null, while false requires all five F3 path/hash fields null. `controller_recoveries` is an immutable array sorted by `(controller_kind,lane_or_form)` of exact `{controller_kind,lane_or_form,journal_path,lease_path,initial_journal_sha256,state,final_journal_sha256,common_receipt_path,common_receipt_sha256}`; kinds/forms are `review-lane:F1|F2` or `final-wave:inputs|scope-pre|pre-f4|final`, state is `pending|journal-sealed|receipt-written|complete`, and only state plus corresponding final hashes advance. A validated terminal-success journal is excluded; a sealed missing-receipt journal is included. Publisher ID/journal/initial hash are nonnull exactly when the final-wave journal binds a live persistent publisher, otherwise all publisher fields are null. `resource_actions` is the exact reverse-topological physical array `{action_id,claim_id,resource_kind,operation,identity_sha256}` with closed mappings `process -> observe-prior-boot-process-absent`, `container -> stop-remove-container`, `borrowed-network-attachment -> detach-borrowed-network`, `owned-network -> remove-owned-network`, `owned-volume -> remove-owned-volume`, `filesystem-staging -> remove-staging`, `candidate-publication-envelope -> publish-or-adopt-candidate-envelope`, and `candidate-publication-history -> publish-or-adopt-candidate-history`; claim removal is not a physical action. For every active candidate publisher with nonnull binding and canonical history authorization unpublished, the action plan unconditionally contains exactly three adjacent actions in this order, even when the staged path is already absent: `candidate-envelope-complete-<claim_id>`, `candidate-staging-complete-<claim_id>`, and `candidate-history-complete-<claim_id>`. Let `C=SHA256(RFC8785(prior_claim))`, `B=SHA256(RFC8785(candidate_envelope_binding))`, `E` be the exact binding-reconstructed published envelope authorization object with its one canonical destination entry, `R` be the exact timestamp-free history record defined above using `B` and `SHA256(RFC8785(E.entries))`, and `H` be the exact published history authorization object whose one entry hashes `RFC8785(R)+LF`. The envelope action's identity object is exactly `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,action_id:"candidate-envelope-complete-<claim_id>",claim_id,resource_kind:"candidate-publication-envelope",operation:"publish-or-adopt-candidate-envelope",origin_claim_sha256:C,candidate_envelope_binding_sha256:B,authorization_id,root_path,relative_path,would_be_observed_authorization:E}`. The staging action's identity object is exactly `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,action_id:"candidate-staging-complete-<claim_id>",claim_id,resource_kind:"filesystem-staging",operation:"remove-staging",origin_claim_sha256:C,candidate_envelope_binding_sha256:B,predecessor_action_id:"candidate-envelope-complete-<claim_id>",claim_root_path:"<attempt_root>/claims/<claim_id>",relative_path:"candidate-envelope.json",expected_staged_entry}`; `expected_staged_entry` is exactly `{relative_path:"candidate-envelope.json",mode:256,uid:<executor-uid>,gid:<executor-gid>,size_bytes:<length-of-RFC8785(binding.envelope)+LF>,sha256:<binding.envelope_sha256>}`. The history action's identity object is exactly `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,action_id:"candidate-history-complete-<claim_id>",claim_id,resource_kind:"candidate-publication-history",operation:"publish-or-adopt-candidate-history",origin_claim_sha256:C,candidate_envelope_binding_sha256:B,predecessor_action_id:"candidate-staging-complete-<claim_id>",envelope_action_id:"candidate-envelope-complete-<claim_id>",authorization_id,root_path,relative_path,predecessor_authorization_id,predecessor_root_path,predecessor_entries_sha256,history_record:R,would_be_observed_authorization:H}`. For each action, `identity_sha256=SHA256(RFC8785(the exact identity object))` with no trailing LF; `E`, `R`, and `H` use the already-closed authorization/entry/history schemas and decimal JSON integers. Thus the same three action IDs and identity hashes are produced whether the staged file is present or absent. If the canonical envelope authorization is already published, `E` must equal its exact observed object; if it is unpublished, destination absent and exact-present-unrecorded are the only legal file prefixes. The envelope action publishes or adopts that exact entry. The staging action then accepts only the exact nofollow regular binding-derived file or exact absence; when present it unlinks and parent-fsyncs it, and when absent it idempotently records completion without inventing a different hash or predecessor chain. Only after that fixed middle action may the history action publish or adopt `R` and `H`. A candidate with both canonical authorizations already published needs no publication actions; a binding-null candidate has no candidate action and is cleanup/prune eligible only with both destinations and staging absent. Missing, duplicate, reordered, nonadjacent, conditionally omitted, unpaired, cross-claim, or identity-drifted candidate actions make the prepared outer journal invalid. `runner_recoveries` is a sorted-unique array by `(action_id,claim_id)` of exact `{action_id,claim_id,origin_status,origin_claim_sha256,runner_creation}` in bijection with every runner-owned `stop-remove-container` action whose prior claim has runner state `intent|prepared|remove-intent`. `origin_status` is its prior `reserved|prepared|active` status and `origin_claim_sha256` hashes the complete RFC-8785 prior claim. At `prepared`, copied `runner_creation` equals the prior object byte-for-byte; membership, IDs, origin fields, and every immutable creation field never change, while only the copied object advances under the existing `intent|prepared -> remove-intent -> removed` or `remove-intent -> removed` matrix. An intent-only exact matched ID may be captured once at copied `remove-intent` and remains null only after authenticated exact absence; a prior remove intent preserves its reason, ID, and timestamp. No entry exists for nonrunners or an already-removed runner with no physical action. `completed_action_ids` is an ordered prefix. Prepared binds the complete prior ledger, action plan, prior/current boots, fresh boot observation, stable lock, shared evidence, equal reboot-stable projection, every controller/F3 input, and optional persistent publisher before mutation. Cleaning executes only the next physical action: prior-boot processes are observed absent without signaling current PIDs; containers—including intent-bound unprepared runners—networks, volumes, and staging paths are fresh-inspected against exact ID/name/labels/config/inode tuples. A next `publish-or-adopt-candidate-envelope` action revalidates the exact prior claim and nonnull immutable binding, reconstructs the canonical envelope and would-be entry, validates an already canonical-published entry or no-replace publishes/byte-validates an absent-or-exact-unrecorded destination, file+parent-fsyncs it, and completes only the outer action. Only after that action and ordered staging removal may `publish-or-adopt-candidate-history` derive the predecessor digest and binding hash, no-replace publish/byte-validate the exact record, file+parent-fsync it, and complete its outer action. Crashes after either destination fsync but before action completion accept only byte-identical replay under the already authenticated outer journal; neither action changes the canonical prior ledger. Preauthorized absence is idempotent, while partial/foreign/recreated identity blocks. Before a runner stop/remove, the outer journal file-and-parent-fsyncs its copied `remove-intent`; only then may it remove the exact bound container or authenticate absence. After the deterministic-name/intent-label union is absent, one atomic outer-journal replacement sets the copied state to `removed` and appends that action ID. A crash accepts only the prior copied state before mutation or the exact next copied state afterward; the canonical ledger is never replaced during this physical sequence. At `resources-absent`, every action is complete, every `runner_recoveries[].runner_creation.state` is `removed`, every binding-nonnull candidate has staging absent and either its canonical envelope/history pair is already published and revalidated with no candidate actions or that pair is durable and byte-valid with all three ordered outer actions complete, every binding-null candidate has both destinations and staging absent, zero task resources exist, and the canonical ledger still hashes exactly to `prior_ledger_sha256`. It fsyncs `claims-prune-intent` with nonnull `post_cleanup_ledger_sha256` computed from that byte-identical prior ledger by removing every nonpublisher claim while retaining the sole exact `reserved|active` persistent publisher claim byte-for-byte when present, or no claims; candidate claims are removable only when binding-null with both destinations and staging absent, or when binding-nonnull with staging absent and either both canonical authorizations were already published and revalidated or the exact ordered envelope/staging/history actions completed; the outer runner tombstones plus completed candidate actions remain immutable. The sole legal atomic replace performs exactly that prune while leaving boot/proof fields unchanged. Recovery accepts only the prior hash before this replace or post-cleanup hash after it, then advances `claims-pruned`. `post_cleanup_ledger_sha256` is null through `resources-absent`, nonnull from `claims-prune-intent` onward, and immutable. `post_update_ledger_sha256` is null through `claims-pruned`; on resume it becomes nonnull exactly at `resume-proof-published`, while on reject it becomes nonnull exactly at `receipts-finalized`, and remains immutable. The two resume-proof fields are nonnull exactly from resume-proof-published through resume complete and always null on reject. `failure_receipts_sha256` is always null on resume and becomes nonnull exactly at reject receipts-finalized. Publisher intent/final hashes are nonnull only from their named reject states when a publisher was bound; all publisher recovery fields are null when no publisher exists. From `claims-pruned`, `resume` requires the controller/F3 arrays and receipt aggregate still empty, no terminal decision, no live resource, and any retained publisher to be the sole exact `reserved|active` claim with byte-valid immutable authorizations; a retained reserved publisher must have a completely unpublished/empty authorization vector. The outer reconciler spawns the committed proof publisher with `pass_fds=(stable_lock_fd,)`; its sole special form `publish --authority-root <absolute-dir> --stale-boot-resume <absolute-mode-0600-journal> --stable-lock-fd <nonnegative-int>` fstat/path-validates that inherited open file description, nonblocking-reasserts the same flock, requires this exact journal at `claims-pruned` with goal resume and exact current boot/resource/baseline/proof-parent observations, rejects every archive marker, and create-or-validates only the new current-boot mode-0400 proof while retaining the prior proof. After the special publisher returns, the outer reconciler nofollow-byte-validates the exact current-boot proof and constructs the complete prospective ledger bytes from the journal-bound closed `boot_observation`, using `boot_observation.observed_at_utc` as `last_verified_at_utc`, binding the new execution-host proof, and preserving plus immutable-file-refreshing the sole publisher in its exact prior `reserved|active` status when present. Stale recovery never activates a reserved publisher. One atomic file-and-parent-fsynced outer-journal replace advances `claims-pruned -> resume-proof-published` while setting both resume-proof fields and the already-computed nonnull `post_update_ledger_sha256`; advancing with any of those fields null is invalid. At `resume-proof-published`, recovery accepts exactly the post-cleanup hash with the preserved claim set and performs the replace, or the post-update hash and advances; every other hash or claim shape blocks. `boot-updated|complete` require the post-update hash. Resume complete authorizes ordinary same-boot claims/product work in this same attempt and forbids reject/close/archive on boot change alone. If the exact empty publisher remains reserved, the first ordinary final-wave entry create-or-validates only its exact empty claim root, atomically activates the ledger claim, and then fsyncs journal state `active`; recovery accepts `journal=reserved,ledger=active` only as that crash-after-ledger-activation prefix and advances after full spec/empty-vector validation, while `journal=active,ledger=reserved` blocks. For `reject`, `claims-pruned` advances to `receipts-finalizing`, completes F3 first and every controller entry in immutable order, and requires at least one common receipt. `f3_recovery_required` and `controller_recoveries` retain the closed path/hash/state schemas below. Changed-boot finalization nofollow-locks the fixed lease, proves each prior-boot child/domain absent without signaling current PIDs, and sets `recovery_boot_id=current_boot_id`. For the sole final-wave `form=final` publisher-canonical F4 `REJECT` prefix whose durable wait result is exit 0 and whose typed semantic outcome plus published authorization are already bound, it preserves `termination_kind="wait-result"`, exit 0, typed-output/F4 hashes, and adds `boot-changed` only as a lower-precedence observed receipt cause. Every other selected prefix seals at `failure-ready` with `termination_kind="boot-disappearance"`, `boot_disappearance_sha256` over authenticated prior/current boot observations, null wait exit/signal/typed-output fields, and `timed_out=false`; a pre-existing sealed journal is never relabeled. Each finalizer hashes that immutable journal, then creates-or-byte-validates its common receipt with nonnull journal hash and records receipt path/hash only in the outer journal. `receipts-finalized` requires every selected journal/receipt immutable, a nonnull canonical sorted `failure_receipts_sha256`, the exact rejection prefix, and a precomputed nonnull `post_update_ledger_sha256`. An active publisher follows the existing rejection-prefix rule. A reserved publisher is never activated on reject: only when its exact UUID/purpose/spec match, it has no reverse dependent or observed resource, its claim root/pending staging are absent after any exact empty-root `remove-staging` action, every authorization is unpublished/empty, and every destination is absent may `reserved -> release-intent` bind the all-empty authorization digest and finalized receipt aggregate. Hash/claim presence distinguish a crash and advance `boot-updated`. A bound publisher is then finished to released, its final hash is bound, and state advances `publisher-released`; without one this state is skipped. Reject complete requires exact post-update ledger bytes, every F3/controller receipt finalized, publisher released when bound, no live claim/resource, and no unbound controller journal; only then may ordinary `rejection-context -> reject -> close -> archive-rollover -> current-boot proof -> snapshot --after-archive` run. New claims/product work stay forbidden in the rejected old attempt. `reconcile --terminal-final` is the sole same- or changed-boot recovery form for a sealed `final-frozen` approval gate, the still-open sealed USER gate, and an exact accepted-close journal at `phase=prepared` while the canonical ledger remains open. Any accepted-close phase at or after `ledger-closed` forbids terminal reconciliation and permits only receipt/phase completion through `close`. Before terminal-artifact publication it may recover only the post-FINAL success prefix and journal-bound publisher. It requires zero nonpublisher claims/resources and either no final-controller record or one exact `form=final` success-compatible record whose durable child wait is `exit_code=0`, typed freezer outcome/hash matches `final-frozen|final.json`, child identity is absent, and fixed lease is free. Under stable-lock-then-final-lock order it revalidates the exact clean SHA/tree/worktree, approved-plan hash, FINAL inventory/hash, approval chain, authorization-state digest, shared evidence, stable lock/projection, and fresh boot observation.
