The terminal approval aggregate is SHA-256 of RFC-8785 canonical JSON for the lane-sorted exact array `{lane,relative_path,sha256,decision:"APPROVE"}` using `F1:terminal/F1-verdict.json`, `F2:terminal/F2-verdict.json`, `F3:terminal/F3/manifest.json`, and `F4:terminal/F4-final.txt`; `final.json` records this `approvals_sha256`. It then create-or-byte-validates mode-0400 `<terminal_revalidation_root>/<current-boot-id>.json` with exact canonical keys `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,purpose:"terminal-final",sha,tree_sha,previous_boot_id,current_boot_id,boot_changed,final_relative_path,final_sha256,approvals_sha256,reboot_stable_baseline_sha256,boot_observation,boot_observation_sha256,shared_evidence_manifest_sha256,lock_identity_sha256,publisher_final_journal_sha256,pre_update_ledger_sha256,post_update_ledger_sha256,accepted_close_prepared_sha256,verified_at_utc}`. `final_relative_path` is exactly `clinic-os-phase1a-final/final.json`; the embedded closed boot observation hashes to `boot_observation_sha256`, `verified_at_utc=boot_observation.observed_at_utc`, and publisher journal hash binds the immutable `released` state. `accepted_close_prepared_sha256` is null when no accepted-close journal exists; it is nonnull only for an exact `phase=prepared` journal with the canonical ledger open and hashes the exact journal bytes read under the stable lock. With such prepared journal `P`, terminal reconciliation accepts the open ledger only when it hashes to `P.preclose_ledger_sha256` or to the post-update hash of the exact ledger-boot terminal artifact whose `accepted_close_prepared_sha256=SHA256(P)` and whose scope/publisher fields equal `P`; this immediate-predecessor rule supports repeated reboots before `P` is rebound. After publisher release, `pre_update_ledger_sha256=Ht0` hashes the exact zero-claim ledger. If the current-boot artifact is absent, terminal reconciliation performs the final refresh itself: after the same-boot full-baseline/current-execution-host-proof checks or changed-boot stable-projection checks, it captures one fresh closed `boot_observation`, requires `boot_observation.observed_at_utc` later than the ledger’s current `last_verified_at_utc`, and constructs `Ht1` by setting `boot_observation` and `last_verified_at_utc=boot_observation.observed_at_utc`; only after a boot change does it also set `boot_id=current_boot_id`. Thus same-boot `Ht1` differs from `Ht0` only in `boot_observation|last_verified_at_utc`, while changed-boot `Ht1` differs only in `boot_id|boot_observation|last_verified_at_utc`. The no-replace artifact binds `Ht0` and `Ht1` and is file-and-parent-fsynced before ledger replacement. Recovery accepts only `Ht0|Ht1`: at `Ht0` it performs the exact replacement; at `Ht1` it reuses the identical artifact bytes and original observation. An existing current-boot artifact is replay-only and never causes another observation or refresh. When a rebound prepared journal directly binds that artifact by `P.terminal_revalidation_sha256=SHA256(T)` and `P.preclose_ledger_sha256=T.post_update_ledger_sha256=current-ledger SHA`, matching scope fields make that direct binding sufficient even though immutable `T.accepted_close_prepared_sha256` hashes the predecessor prepared generation; otherwise the accepted-close field must hash the exact prepared generation it supersedes. The command returns only at `Ht1`, with zero claims/resources, publisher `released`, and fresh terminal equality validated, printing exactly the nofollow-validated absolute artifact path plus LF. Once the artifact is durable, `verify --refresh` and every other ordinary open-ledger replacement are forbidden; only terminal replay, USER sidecar/spec publication, and the applicable atomic close remain legal. It creates no current-boot execution-host proof, retains the prior proof, and never mutates FINAL. While the ledger remains open, an exact USER intent, exact USER-only rejection spec, or exact prepared accepted-close journal keeps this terminal form legal after every reboot and before close; ordinary consumers and nonsealed stale recovery remain forbidden. Stable identity, approval, artifact, publisher, ledger-hash, stale-cleanup-journal, or baseline drift hard-blocks close and USER rejection.
- Archive-rollover recovery contract: while continuously holding and revalidating the stable lock, `archive-rollover` first validates the closed ledger, the exact current rejection spec, the absent-or-schema-allowed control root, the complete attempt root, and the no-replace destination; computes the complete source identities and `control_tree_sha256|attempt_tree_sha256|ledger_sha256`; and no-replace creates plus file/parent-fsyncs mode-0600 `.omo/evidence/isolation-archive-rollover-phase1a.json` with exact keys `{schema_version,attempt_id,rejected_sha,rejection_spec_sha256,bundle_path,stable_lock_identity_sha256,archive_identity_sha256,state,control_tree_sha256,attempt_tree_sha256,ledger_sha256,tombstone_sha256,updated_at_utc}` and `state="prepared|control-moved|attempt-moved|ledger-moved|tombstone-published|relocating|complete"`. The CLI argument is the expected spec hash, never a path. In `prepared`, every source hash is already nonnull except `control_tree_sha256=null` only for the separately authenticated pre-control ORCH case, and `tombstone_sha256=null`; no source has moved. Only after that exact prepared journal is durable does it no-replace create plus file/parent-fsync the stable mode-0600 sentinel `.omo/evidence/isolation-archive-rollover-phase1a.sentinel` with exact immutable keys `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,rejected_sha,bundle_path,archive_identity_sha256,stable_lock_identity_sha256}`. Both artifacts carry the same immutable `archive_identity_sha256`, which hashes canonical `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,rejected_sha,rejection_spec_sha256,bundle_path,stable_lock_identity_sha256,control_tree_sha256,attempt_tree_sha256,ledger_sha256}` and therefore remains verifiable after mutable journal state advances. No rename is legal until both artifacts validate. The closed initial recovery matrix is: neither path and no moved/bundled prefix permits a fresh start; a valid stable `prepared` journal with no sentinel is the sole pre-sentinel crash state and may only revalidate every still-unmoved source/destination/hash then create that exact sentinel; a valid stable `prepared` journal plus matching sentinel proceeds; a sentinel without a stable or bundled journal carrying that exact archive identity is impossible and blocks without mutation. For every later state it derives exactly one legal spec locator: `<attempt_root>/rejection-spec.json` before the attempt rename or `<bundle>/attempt/rejection-spec.json` after it; exactly one must exist and hash-match, while both/neither or any other location rejects. The journal-location table is closed: `prepared|control-moved|attempt-moved|ledger-moved|tombstone-published` exists only at the stable sibling and bundled `archive-state.json` is absent; `relocating` exists at exactly one of those two paths with byte-identical content; `complete` exists only as `<bundle>/archive-state.json` and the stable sibling is absent. Every no-replace resource rename plus parent fsync advances and fsyncs the stable journal while preserving the sentinel-bound stable-lock/archive identity and attempt/destination/source hashes. After tombstone validation, it atomically replaces/fsyncs the stable journal with `state="relocating"`, no-replace renames that exact inode to `<bundle>/archive-state.json`, fsyncs both source and bundle parents, then atomically replaces/fsyncs only the bundled journal with `state="complete"`; it never moves a complete journal. The sentinel remains at its stable path through every relocation state, lets recovery locate and authenticate the bundled journal after the stable journal moves, and is unlinked plus parent-fsynced only after bundled `complete` and every bundle hash/fsync validate. A crash after bundled completion but before sentinel removal may only revalidate `complete` and remove that same sentinel. A crash before the journal rename leaves stable `relocating`; after rename but before either parent fsync leaves bundled `relocating`; after parent fsync but before the final replace also leaves bundled `relocating`; recovery validates the exact location/content and completes the next transition. Any absent/replaced/mismatched sentinel after prepared and before bundled completion, both-journal-path, no-journal-path other than terminal bundled `complete` after sentinel cleanup, wrong-state/location, replaced inode, source hash/identity drift, or completed-state mismatch fails closed. Both the embedded pre-edit proof command and committed proof publisher serialize on the retained stable lock when it exists, check the stable archive journal and sentinel before both proof creation and same-boot reuse and immediately before output, and reject while either exists, so every create/rename/fsync boundary has one deterministic oracle and no proof can overlap archive rollover.
- Accepted-close recovery contract: while the canonical ledger is open, on the creation boot the exact form is `close --final-receipt .omo/evidence/isolation-ledger-final-phase1a.json`; after changed-boot terminal reconciliation the exact form is `close --terminal-revalidation <authority-root>/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-runtime/<attempt-id>/terminal-revalidation/<current-boot-id>.json --final-receipt .omo/evidence/isolation-ledger-final-phase1a.json`. Once an authenticated accepted-close journal binds the exact closed ledger, the exact receipt-only form is `close --final-receipt .omo/evidence/isolation-ledger-final-phase1a.json`; it may nofollow-validate an explicitly supplied historical terminal path but never requires or creates a current-boot artifact. Both use mode-0600 `<attempt_root>/accepted-close-state.json` under the unchanged stable lock with exact keys `{schema_version,attempt_id,phase,sha,tree_sha,final_sha256,inputs_sha256,pre_f4_sha256,approvals_sha256,reboot_stable_baseline_sha256,terminal_revalidation_relative_path,terminal_revalidation_sha256,preclose_ledger_sha256,closed_ledger_sha256,receipt_sha256,closed_at_utc,updated_at_utc}` and phase `prepared|ledger-closed|receipt-published|complete`. `terminal_revalidation_relative_path` is exactly `terminal-revalidation/<preparation-boot-id>.json`: while the canonical ledger is open it must name the current-boot artifact for the prepared generation; after the ledger equals `closed_ledger_sha256` it remains the immutable historical closure-boot binding and no new current-boot artifact is required. Preparation nofollow-opens that mode-0400 file, revalidates its exact terminal schema/purpose/attempt/SHA/tree/FINAL/approval/stable-baseline/current-boot hashes, and derives `terminal_revalidation_sha256` from its bytes. It independently recomputes `approvals_sha256` from the exact four-lane canonical array above and requires equality with both `final.json` and terminal revalidation. Preparation also requires the current open ledger SHA-256 to equal the terminal artifact's `post_update_ledger_sha256`, `claims=[]`, zero task resources, `rejection_close=null`, and the exact final-wave journal at `terminal_publisher_state=released` with SHA-256 equal to `publisher_final_journal_sha256`; a pre-update ledger, active/release-intent publisher, or journal/hash drift fails before close mutation. Both boot branches consume the final refresh already completed by terminal reconciliation and perform no ordinary ledger mutation between terminal-artifact publication and close. Creation-boot close canonically discovers the current artifact and omits `--terminal-revalidation`; changed-boot close requires the captured absolute path above and retains the immutable prior-boot execution-host proof. In both cases `preclose_ledger_sha256` equals the artifact’s `post_update_ledger_sha256`; omission/substitution of the changed-boot flag or any intervening ledger mutation fails before close mutation. After terminal reconciliation and explicit user acceptance it computes one `closed_at_utc` plus the exact closed-ledger and receipt bytes, O_CREAT|O_EXCL no-replace/file+parent-fsyncs the initial `prepared` journal, atomically replaces/fsyncs the canonical ledger with only those closed bytes, advances/validates `ledger-closed`, no-replace publishes/fsyncs the receipt, then advances `receipt-published|complete`; it never publishes the receipt first and never reopens a closed ledger. Explicit acceptance authorizes that initial prepared generation; every later valid prepared generation has the identical immutable target and needs no second user prompt. If a reboot leaves exact prepared journal `Pold`, the ledger open, and the receipt absent, terminal reconciliation first creates/replays current artifact `T` under the predecessor rules above. `close` reuses `Pold` byte-for-byte when it already directly binds `T` and `current-ledger SHA=Pold.preclose_ledger_sha256=T.post_update_ledger_sha256`. Otherwise it may perform the sole same-phase `prepared(old) -> prepared(new)` transition only when `T` is the nofollow-validated current-boot artifact, `T.accepted_close_prepared_sha256=SHA256(Pold)`, the ledger hashes to `T.post_update_ledger_sha256`, the receipt is absent, and all immutable attempt/SHA/tree/FINAL/input/pre-F4/approval/baseline/publisher fields match. `Pnew` preserves exactly `{schema_version,attempt_id,phase:"prepared",sha,tree_sha,final_sha256,inputs_sha256,pre_f4_sha256,approvals_sha256,reboot_stable_baseline_sha256}`; it replaces only terminal relative path/hash, `preclose_ledger_sha256`, one newly computed `closed_at_utc`, the closed-ledger/receipt hashes computed solely from those current open bytes, and `updated_at_utc`, using same-directory mode-0600 temp, file fsync, atomic replace, and parent fsync. A same-boot replay of `Pnew` validates identical bytes and never generates another timestamp. Once the canonical ledger equals the prepared `closed_ledger_sha256`, every prepared field is frozen permanently. The mode-0400 receipt is exact canonical JSON `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,sha,tree_sha,final_sha256,inputs_sha256,pre_f4_sha256,approvals_sha256,reboot_stable_baseline_sha256,terminal_revalidation_sha256,closed_ledger_sha256,closed_at_utc}`. The replay matrix is closed: journal absent plus open ledger permits only initial preparation from the current terminal artifact; journal absent plus closed ledger or receipt fails. Prepared plus open ledger and absent receipt requires exact direct P/T/preclose equality or terminal reconciliation followed by the authorized prepared-to-prepared replacement above; an incomplete terminal update at its pre-hash refuses close. Prepared plus ledger bytes exactly equal to its `closed_ledger_sha256` is the sole crash-after-ledger-replace prefix and advances to `ledger-closed` without reconciliation or rebinding. `ledger-closed` plus exact closed ledger and absent receipt publishes the exact receipt; an already exact receipt advances. `receipt-published|complete` requires the exact closed ledger and receipt and only finishes/replays the phase. On an interrupted-close restart, `close` classifies journal/ledger under the stable lock: an open prefix follows reconcile-current-artifact-close, while an exact closed prefix runs receipt-only `close --final-receipt` with no new artifact or ledger write; a supplied terminal path in receipt-only recovery must equal the journal's historical binding. Any open ledger at phase `ledger-closed|receipt-published|complete`, receipt with open ledger, prepared+closed hash mismatch, closed ledger without its prepared journal, mismatched receipt, stale/current artifact mismatch while open, prepared replacement after closure, or source/output hash drift fails closed.
- Shared-evidence protection: after the namespace bootstrap records `.omo` and `.omo/evidence`, first-attempt snapshot requires the exact current attempt root, current rejection root, final control root, approved copy/sidecar, ledger, archive-rollover state, and final receipt absent; retry snapshot validates and reuses the immutable approved copy/sidecar after a completed archive. It recursively inventories every pre-existing child beneath the resolved `.omo/evidence` without following symlinks, recording sorted relative path/type/mode/uid/gid/size/SHA-256 for regular files or link text for symlinks into the mode-0400 shared baseline. The only exclusions are the exact canonical ledger and stable lock, approved copy and sidecar, this attempt’s exact `clinic-os-phase1a-runtime/<attempt_id>/`, this attempt’s exact future `clinic-os-phase1a-rejected/<attempt_id>/`, the current fixed `clinic-os-phase1a-final/`, active `.omo/evidence/isolation-archive-rollover-phase1a.json`, and `.omo/evidence/isolation-ledger-final-phase1a.json`; no broad prefix, prior runtime path, or completed rejection bundle is excluded. Todo receipts/captures live only beneath the current `todo_evidence_root`, so rejection archives them with the attempt. Existing collisions at any current excluded path fail preflight. After archive, `snapshot --after-archive` inventories the entire completed rejection bundle—including its control tree when present, attempt evidence, closed ledger, tombstone, and receipt-lineage seed plus final lineage when present—as protected immutable history while excluding only the newly selected attempt paths; only the lineage resolver may read the exact hashed receipt paths. Every stage verifies the unrelated baseline before and after execution. During F1, F2, F4, and every post-claim F3 stage/helper subprocess, each child receives `CLINIC_EVIDENCE_ROOT=<claim_root>/evidence` and may write only there or through a schema-valid published-output authorization. The pre-claim `final_e2e.sh` and outside-parent `final_e2e_supervisor.py` are the sole F3 environment exception: they receive no ambient evidence-root variable. The shell receives only the exact final-E2E outer argv, validates launcher/input identities, and execs the supervisor. Input-frozen supervisor arguments bind the nofollow ledger, stable-lock, proof, authority, attempt-root, failure-receipt, control/evidence, and cgroup-parent paths and identities; only the supervisor may use them for ledger RPC execution, journal/kill-domain state, cleanup, final-lock publication, and the post-cleanup common receipt. The barrier-moved persistent controller receives no ledger/stable-lock/proof/authority/failure-receipt/control/evidence/cgroup-parent path, FD, or environment capability: before orchestration activation it receives only input hashes, attempt/claim IDs, its exact descendant-cgroup identity, and authenticated supervisor RPC; afterward it additionally receives its claim-owned staging root. It cannot open a fixed final/attempt destination, and descendant stages/helpers receive only their exact claim-owned evidence root. The failure receipt remains a stable-lock-governed attempt artifact after claim release, never a final-terminal published output. After orchestration-claim activation all spawned stage/helper children regain the mandatory variable; frozen current-attempt todo/fix receipts are read-only inputs. Historical bundles and siblings are never renamed, rewritten, or removed, and any namespace-lstat or Merkle/metadata drift fails.
- Normative sidecar and receipt closure: the local approved-plan sidecar is exactly `.omo/evidence/review-inputs/approved-plan.sha256`; the exact primary receipt files are `<todo_evidence_root>/task-<N>-clinic-os-phase-1a-staff-scheduling.json` and exact capture directories are `<todo_evidence_root>/task-<N>-clinic-os-phase-1a-staff-scheduling/` for `N=1..20`, created inside the primary attempt and never at a fixed cross-attempt path; later attempts resolve those immutable archived receipts only through the authenticated lineage contract below. In every receipt, `argv` is an exact JSON array of strings, paths are sorted workspace-relative POSIX paths, node-ID arrays are sorted and unique, timestamps use the schema-v2 UTC format, and all hashes are lowercase SHA-256. Green is exactly `exit_code=0`, `classification=pass`, the same argv and target node IDs as red, and an empty `observed_failed_nodeids`; red ends before green starts. Each `tests_after_entries[]` is exactly `{test_paths,test_source_sha256,argv,started_at_utc,ended_at_utc,exit_code,classification,http_contract_nodeids,stdout_sha256,stderr_sha256}`, requires exit 0 plus `classification=visual-polish-no-behavior`, and points to an already-green HTTP-contract node set. Each `acceptance[]` is exactly `{argv,started_at_utc,ended_at_utc,exit_code,stdout_sha256,stderr_sha256}`; each `resources[]` is exactly `{claim_id,kind,activated_at_utc,released_at_utc}`; and `cleanup` is exactly `{verified_at_utc,claim_ids_absent,paths_absent,containers_absent,volumes_absent,networks_absent,listeners_absent,process_members_absent,shared_evidence_manifest_sha256}` with every absence field boolean true. The validator rejects any extra/missing key, records empty arrays for inapplicable categories, proves every test blob at `test_source_sha256` is present unchanged in `primary_commit_sha`, and verifies that commit's sole parent is `parent_sha`.
- Supplemental review-fix receipts: every additive post-rejection commit has exactly one `<todo_evidence_root>/review-fix-<sequence>.json`, with positive globally contiguous sequence across archived lineage plus the current attempt and exact keys `{schema_version,sequence,change_class,owning_todo,review_lane,rejected_sha,parent_sha,fix_commit_sha,changed_paths,tdd_entries,tests_after_entries,acceptance,resources,cleanup}`. `change_class` is exactly `behavior|tests|visual-polish|docs-only`; `review_lane` is `F1|F2|F3|F4|ORCH|USER`; `ORCH` is legal only when the authenticated originating ORCH receipt itself has `retry_kind=source-fix`, while a semantic F4 rejection always uses `F4`; the commit has sole parent `parent_sha`, exact diff paths equal sorted unique `changed_paths`, and exact `Owning-todo`, `Review-lane`, and `Rejected-SHA` trailers. Behavior/test fixes require nonempty machine-bound red/green entries; visual-only fixes require nonempty tests-after entries tied to an already-green HTTP contract; docs-only fixes require empty test arrays, Markdown/docs-only diff paths, and explicit link plus `git diff --check` acceptance. The same nested schemas, source digest, chronology, redaction, resource, and cleanup rules apply. Before every final freeze, Todo 19 validates a gap-free cross-attempt receipt chain covering every commit after the twenty primary commits through the candidate SHA; an unreceipted, multiply receipted, wrong-parent, wrong-class, or wrong-rejected-SHA commit rejects. An evidence-only rejected attempt contains no source commit or supplemental receipt, does not advance `next_fix_sequence`, and is valid only through the immutable rejection/lineage contract below.
- Cross-attempt receipt lineage: every snapshot creates mode-0400 `<attempt_root>/receipt-lineage-seed.json` with exact keys `{schema_version,current_attempt_id,previous_attempt_id,previous_bundle_path,previous_tombstone_sha256,previous_lineage_sha256,primary_sources,fix_sources,next_fix_sequence}`. On the first attempt, previous fields are null, source arrays are empty, and `next_fix_sequence=1`. Each primary source is exactly `{todo,attempt_id,bundle_path,relative_path,sha256,primary_commit_sha}`; each fix source is exactly `{sequence,attempt_id,bundle_path,relative_path,sha256,fix_commit_sha}`. `bundle_path` is nullable with closed logical-locator semantics: it is null only when `attempt_id` equals the lineage `current_attempt_id`, and resolves against that lineage container attempt root (`<active-attempt-root>/<relative_path>` before archive or `<bundle>/attempt/<relative_path>` while verifying an archived lineage); an older source must instead carry the exact workspace-relative POSIX bundle path `.omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-rejected/<attempt_id>/<rejected_sha>` and resolves only as `<worktree>/<bundle_path>/attempt/<relative_path>`. A nonnull current-attempt locator, a null older-attempt locator, path traversal, or attempt/path mismatch rejects. `snapshot --after-archive` always verifies the prior tombstone's attempt tree and nonnull `receipt_lineage_seed_sha256`. When `receipt_lineage_sha256` is nonnull it verifies that final lineage normally; when it is null—which is legal only for an authenticated `ORCH-input-freeze` publication-stage infrastructure/timeout/signal failure after the exact durable lineage-validation checkpoint was no-replace published and fsynced but before atomic final-lineage publication—it verifies the archived checkpoint and its tombstone/receipt hash, the archived seed, re-runs the closed resolver over the exact schema-valid primary/fix receipt paths inside the already hashed archived attempt tree, requires the same 20-primary/gap-free-fix rules, and derives the immutable source arrays directly from those paths. For both an ordinary published lineage and the narrow null-lineage fallback, it translates every just-archived source whose `attempt_id` equals that archived attempt and whose `bundle_path` is null into the exact immutable bundle path while preserving `relative_path`; it rejects any other null locator, recursively verifies every older source through its hashed bundle/tombstone chain, and no-replace creates the new seed; repeated rejection, including evidence-only unchanged-SHA attempts, never copies or rewrites a receipt. Before creating the final-wave journal, bootstrap root, or control root, Todo 19 holds the stable ledger lock, combines that seed with exactly 20 current primary receipts on the first attempt or no new primaries later, plus current-attempt fixes starting at `next_fix_sequence` (or none for an evidence-only attempt), and first canonicalizes the exact validated candidate `{schema_version:1,current_attempt_id,seed_sha256,primary_sources,fix_sources,next_fix_sequence}`. It no-replace writes, file+parent-fsyncs, and re-hashes those exact bytes as mode-0400 `<attempt_root>/receipt-lineage-validation.json`; only then does it no-replace write and fsync mode-0400 `<attempt_root>/receipt-lineage.json` with exact keys `{schema_version,current_attempt_id,seed_sha256,lineage_validation_sha256,primary_sources,fix_sources,next_fix_sequence}`, where `lineage_validation_sha256` binds that checkpoint. The resolver requires exactly one receipt for each primary todo, a globally gap-free fix sequence whose commits cover every post-primary commit through candidate SHA, matching approved-plan/foundation/parent/trailer/source hashes, and byte/hash/tree equality at every archived source. Missing, duplicate, copied, mutable, wrong-bundle, or skipped-attempt lineage fails before final freeze. Every primary and supplemental receipt has already passed the identical Todo-1-owned closed validator in mutable claim staging before immutable publication. A deterministic source validation failure or staged-receipt validation failure occurs before publication/final freeze, creates no ORCH receipt or archive, and is repaired under the ordinary owning-todo evidence/source rules before setting the final candidate SHA. A later failure of those same bytes under the same validator hash proves immutable tamper, validator mismatch, or lineage corruption: it hard-blocks final freeze/rejection/archive for forensic resolution and is never treated as an ordinarily replaceable published receipt. Only after the durable checkpoint exists and revalidates may a publication-stage I/O/timeout/signal failure before atomic final-lineage publication enter the authenticated ORCH receipt plus seed-backed archive fallback above. If the child dies before checkpoint publication, the outer controller must rerun the closed resolver under the stable lock and publish/revalidate the identical checkpoint before it can write any ORCH receipt; failed or nondeterministic revalidation blocks receipt, rejection, and archive. After final-lineage publication every journal records both checkpoint and lineage hashes. The rejection tombstone always authenticates the seed and lineage-validation checkpoint hashes and authenticates the final-lineage hash when present.
- Rejection and unchanged-SHA retry contract: under the stable ledger lock and only after every lane process/resource/claim is absent and the shared baseline matches, `reject` no-replace creates canonical mode-0400 `<attempt_root>/rejection-spec.json` with exact keys `{schema_version,attempt_id,sha,retry_kind,rejections,failure_receipts_sha256,user_fix_intent_sha256,inputs_sha256,pre_f4_sha256,final_sha256}`. Each sorted unique `rejections[]` entry is exactly `{lane,failure_class}`; `lane=F1|F2|F3|F4|ORCH|USER`, and `failure_class=finding|product-failure|infrastructure|authentication|session|timeout|malformed-output|user-requested-fix`. Repeated `--reason` arguments must equal, byte-for-byte as a sorted set, the authenticated F1-F4/ORCH failure receipts below; they are never free-form source data. The transition permits `USER:user-requested-fix` only when `reject --user-authorization` names the current-boot binding and independently validates that binding, its boot-stable intent, current terminal artifact, approval digest, zero failure receipts, and current open-ledger SHA equality with the artifact’s `post_update_ledger_sha256`; it never infers a lane/class from missing files or accepts a bare USER reason. `failure_receipts_sha256` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the RFC-8785 canonical sorted array of exact `{lane,relative_path,sha256}` entries for every authenticated non-USER receipt; it is null only for USER-only rejection and otherwise nonnull. `user_fix_intent_sha256` is the lowercase SHA-256 of the boot-stable intent for USER and null otherwise; the immutable rejection spec never hashes a boot binding or terminal artifact, so `reject` create-or-byte-validates identical spec bytes and prints the same lowercase spec hash after stdout loss or a reboot-created current binding. USER cannot coexist with any non-USER receipt/reason. It derives `retry_kind=evidence-only` only when every class is `infrastructure|authentication|session|timeout|malformed-output`, no reviewer finding or product assertion failed, and the clean candidate SHA/tree is unchanged; every `finding|product-failure|user-requested-fix` derives `source-fix`. Phase hashes are lowercase SHA-256 or null exactly when the artifact does not yet exist. Before atomically closing an open USER rejection, `close --user-authorization` requires the current binding and independently requires the current open-ledger SHA-256 to equal the consumed artifact’s `post_update_ledger_sha256`; `--terminal-revalidation` is forbidden when that binding says `boot_changed=false` and mandatory with exact path equality when true. The closed-ledger replacement stores `rejection_close` with the spec hash, stable intent path/hash, exact binding path/hash, and terminal path/hash actually consumed. A reboot before close appends a new current binding and idempotently revalidates the unchanged spec; a reboot after atomic close validates the stored historical close evidence without reopening or rebinding. On an already-closed rejected ledger, omitted boot-scoped arguments are legal recovery input and supplied arguments must equal stored close context; recovery reconstructs the unique preclose root only by restoring `state=open`, `closed_at_utc=null`, and `rejection_close=null`, and requires that reconstructed SHA-256 to equal the consumed terminal artifact’s `post_update_ledger_sha256`. After close, `archive-rollover --rejection-spec-sha256 <64hex>` resolves only the journal-bound current-attempt or bundled-attempt locator, re-hashes the spec, validates `user_fix_intent_sha256` against it and `user_fix_binding_sha256|terminal_revalidation_sha256` against closed-ledger `rejection_close`, accepts the corresponding final-wave prefix, moves it with the attempt/ledger, and writes a tombstone containing `rejection_spec_sha256`, `failure_receipts_sha256`, `user_fix_intent_sha256`, `user_fix_binding_sha256`, `terminal_revalidation_sha256`, `retry_kind`, and exact reasons. Those three USER hashes are all null for non-USER and all nonnull for USER; prior binding files remain covered by `attempt_tree_sha256` and need not describe the archive boot. After mandatory `snapshot --after-archive`, an evidence-only retry reruns the entire final wave at the identical SHA with no commit/fix receipt and unchanged fix sequence; a source-fix retry must add the narrow owning-todo commit and supplemental receipt before rerunning. Tests cover mixed reasons, ORCH, USER, missing/forged receipts, multi-cause precedence, forbidden evidence-only classification for every untyped nonzero, repeated unchanged-SHA timeouts, and evidence-only then source-fix lineage. USER fixtures SIGKILL/reboot after intent publication/fsync, binding publication/fsync and before stdout, context, rejection-spec publication and before stdout, reject before close, and immediately before/after closed-ledger replacement; they prove one immutable intent, at most one byte-identical binding per boot, append-only cross-boot bindings, stale-binding rejection, unchanged spec SHA across reboots, field 8 current-boot behavior, and tombstone equality with the binding consumed by close.
- Final-wave failure-receipt and class-mapping contract: first-attempt and retry `snapshot` no-replace create exact mode-0700 `<attempt_root>/final-failure-receipts/` before any final-wave bootstrap. After the named child process has terminated, its claims/resources are absent, and the shared baseline is restored, the controller holds the stable ledger lock and no-replace writes at most one canonical mode-0400 `<failure_receipt_root>/<lane>.json` for each failing `lane=F1|F2|F3|F4|ORCH`; success creates no failure receipt. For lane F3, `controller` means the still-alive `final_e2e_supervisor.py` or its journal-bound `f3_recovery.py` continuation; `final_e2e_controller.py` is always the named stage child and can never create the common receipt. Each receipt has exact keys `{schema_version,attempt_id,sha,lane,stage,observed_causes,cause_code,failure_class,started_at_utc,ended_at_utc,exit_code,signal,timed_out,output_sha256,lineage_validation_sha256,journal_sha256,control_tree_sha256,cleanup_verified}`; timestamps are canonical UTC, exit code/signal and the four hashes are nullable only when not produced, `cleanup_verified=true`, and every available hash binds the exact redacted terminal output, durable lineage-validation checkpoint, current journal, or bounded control tree observed after termination. `lineage_validation_sha256` is mandatory for an `ORCH-input-freeze` receipt that authorizes a null final-lineage archive, and otherwise is null or the exact already-published checkpoint hash. `journal_sha256` is mandatory for every F1/F2/F3 receipt and every F4/ORCH receipt whose final-wave controller journal was published; it hashes the complete immutable mode-0400 terminal `failure-ready` journal. Receipt creation is strictly one-way: the controller first terminates and cleans the child/resources, closes and releases its fixed lease owner, fsyncs `failure-ready`, seals and re-hashes the journal, and only then no-replace creates the common receipt referencing that hash. No journal field contains the common-receipt hash and no journal byte may change after receipt creation. Recovery that finds a valid sealed `failure-ready` journal with no receipt creates the one deterministic receipt; an existing receipt must byte/hash-validate against that sealed journal. `stage` is exactly `F1-review|F2-prerequisites|F2-review|F3-e2e|F4-precheck|F4-final|ORCH-input-freeze|ORCH-pre-f4-freeze|ORCH-final-gate`. `observed_causes` is a nonempty unique array sorted by this exact highest-to-lowest precedence: `reviewer-reject`, `product-assertion`, `auth-rejected`, `malformed-verdict`, `malformed-receipt`, `deadline-exceeded`, `session-reused`, `session-disconnected`, `session-missing`, `auth-unavailable`, `launcher-unavailable`, `dependency-unavailable`, `io-failure`, `unexpected-signal`, `boot-changed`, `cleanup-failure`; `cause_code` must equal its first member, so a semantic rejection/assertion always dominates a later cleanup failure. The closed causal mapping is: `reviewer-reject -> finding`; `product-assertion -> product-failure`; `launcher-unavailable|dependency-unavailable|io-failure|unexpected-signal|boot-changed|cleanup-failure -> infrastructure`; `auth-unavailable|auth-rejected -> authentication`; `session-missing|session-reused|session-disconnected -> session`; `deadline-exceeded -> timeout` with `timed_out=true`; and `malformed-verdict|malformed-receipt -> malformed-output`. No other cause/class pair validates. Outcome derivation is closed: a schema-valid F1/F2 reviewer REJECT contributes `reviewer-reject`. An F4 decision becomes canonical only after the persistent publisher records the matching published `f4-final` authorization: canonical literal REJECT contributes lane `F4`, stage `F4-final`, cause `product-assertion`, and `failure_class=product-failure`, so rejection derives `retry_kind=source-fix` and a repair commit uses `review_lane=F4`. Launch/auth/session/I/O/timeout/signal/malformed-output or incomplete/malformed staging before F4 publication belongs to lane ORCH at `ORCH-final-gate` and leaves F4 absent. A publication or final-freezer failure after canonical APPROVE also maps to `ORCH-final-gate`, retaining the approving F4 prefix and no `final.json`; canonical REJECT is never relabeled ORCH after controller death or boot change. A successfully executed deterministic F2/F3/F4/ORCH validator that terminates nonzero without timeout/signal and without a schema-valid typed infrastructure record contributes `product-assertion`; only an `execve` ENOENT/EACCES record contributes `launcher-unavailable`; only a schema-valid dependency preflight record contributes `dependency-unavailable`; controller I/O exceptions contribute `io-failure`; supervisor deadline expiry contributes `deadline-exceeded`; any other terminating signal contributes `unexpected-signal`; review/session/auth validators contribute only their exact named causes; and a stage-cleanup contract miss appends `cleanup-failure` after the outer controller eventually restores absence/baseline. An untyped child exit can therefore never be classified as evidence-only. `boot-changed` is emitted only by authenticated stale-boot recovery. For F1/F2/F3 and every noncanonical final-wave prefix, the sealed controller journal has `termination_kind="boot-disappearance"` and the receipt has `exit_code=null`, `signal=null`, `timed_out=false`, `output_sha256=null`, a nonnull journal and lineage-validation hash, and `failure_class=infrastructure`. For the sole publisher-canonical F4 `REJECT` prefix, the sealed journal instead preserves `termination_kind="wait-result"`, exit 0, the typed-output and published-F4 hashes; its receipt preserves `cause_code=product-assertion` and `failure_class=product-failure` while appending `boot-changed` only below `product-assertion` in `observed_causes`. Existing sealed failure-ready journals retain their original cause. A later cleanup miss appends lower-precedence `cleanup-failure` without replacing either semantic cause or `boot-changed`. Timeout before output is represented with nullable `output_sha256`; an ORCH bootstrap failure may have null journal/control hashes. Later ORCH stages bind every artifact that exists. Death before checkpoint publication never authorizes a receipt by itself: the outer controller must rerun the resolver and durably publish/revalidate the checkpoint under the stable lock; inability to do so blocks receipt and archive. The receipt producer derives `observed_causes`, effective `cause_code`, and class from wait status; for ordinary changed-boot disappearance it derives them from the outer journal’s authenticated prior/current boot observations plus `termination_kind=boot-disappearance`, the exact invoked stage, closed typed validator records, and post-cleanup hashes; for the sole publisher-canonical F4 `REJECT` preservation it combines the durable wait-result, typed semantic outcome, and published-F4 hash with those authenticated boot observations and cleanup hashes. No branch accepts caller prose. `cleanup_verified=true` means outer recovery ultimately proved absence and baseline equality; if that cannot be proved, rejection/archive is blocked and no common receipt is finalized. `reject` verifies mode/path/hash/schema/mapping/attempt/SHA and requires its non-USER reasons to equal all and only these receipts; a missing, duplicate, pre-termination, mutable, wrong-stage, wrong-hash, wrong-class, or uncleared receipt rejects. Tests cover timeout-before-output, publisher-canonical semantic F4 `REJECT` -> one `F4-final` product-failure receipt and source-fix mapping, pre-publication final-gate failure -> one `ORCH-final-gate` receipt with no F4, approving F4 plus freezer failure -> one `ORCH-final-gate` receipt with the F4 prefix and no `final.json`, ORCH failure before control-root creation, journal/control mismatch, reviewer-reject plus cleanup-failure precedence, product assertion plus cleanup failure, untyped F2/F3 nonzero -> product-assertion, unexpected signal, boot disappearance with null wait/output fields, mixed lanes/classes, forged/reordered observed causes, and identical-SHA evidence-only retry.
- TDD source digest: for sorted `test_paths`, `test_source_sha256` is SHA-256 over the byte concatenation of each UTF-8 POSIX path, one NUL byte, its regular-file length as an unsigned 64-bit big-endian integer, and the exact file bytes. Red, green, and the corresponding committed blobs must reproduce the same digest; symlinks, duplicate paths, missing blobs, path traversal, or byte drift reject.

Before runner activation, only the fixed bootstrap-attestation prelude may execute; no secret, browser, suite, product, or other control frame is sent or accepted until the host's activation acknowledgement, and the server proves `current_user=clinic_app` rather than using owner-backed `live_server`.
