- Host Gunicorn fork/exec argv contract: `browser_server.sh` nofollow-lstats and hashes the absolute task launcher `<worktree>/.venv/bin/python`, resolves/hashes its executable target, validates all fixed inputs, and immediately `exec`s `browser_server_supervisor.py`; it never starts Gunicorn or remains an orchestration parent. The supervisor is the only process allowed to create the Gunicorn master and does so with explicit `os.fork`, the two-phase barrier below, and final `os.execve(<absolute-venv-launcher>, argv, closed_environment)`, never `subprocess.Popen`, `preexec_fn`, a shell, or the Gunicorn console script. Exact argv is `[<absolute-venv-launcher>,"-m","gunicorn","--config","<absolute-worktree>/ops/container/gunicorn_no_proxy.py","--preload","--bind","127.0.0.1:<reserved-port>","--workers","2","--threads","1","--timeout","30","--graceful-timeout","30","--keep-alive","5","--max-requests","0","--access-logfile","-","--error-logfile","-","config.wsgi:application"]`; the reserved digest hashes `b"\\0".join(os.fsencode(arg) for arg in argv) + b"\\0"`. The child calls `setsid`, so its PID equals SID and PGID; exactly two direct workers later share that PGID and listener inode. The pinned environment excludes `setproctitle`, so master/workers retain the exact argv digest; churn or drift closes readiness. The committed config has exactly `forwarded_allow_ips = ""` and `secure_scheme_headers = {}` plus final LF, is clean-source/hash-bound in the process claim, and the environment forbids `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS`/`GUNICORN_CMD_ARGS`. Loopback cleartext tests send every combination of the three supported forwarded-scheme headers and require `wsgi.url_scheme="http"` plus HTTPS redirect; direct Gunicorn TLS derives `https` only from transport.
- Host Gunicorn supervisor/controller hierarchy: Todo 14 adds committed `browser_server_supervisor.py`, `browser_server_controller.py`, and `browser_server_recovery.py`. The shell execs the supervisor, which sets `PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER`, nofollow-creates the durable journal, allocates/fsyncs the canonical Gunicorn process-claim UUID, creates the kill domain while itself remaining in the proof-bound delegated parent, and remains the sole fork/direct-parent/reap/journal/ledger owner. It first forks the controller through an identity/membership/release barrier and places it in that domain; the controller owns dependency orchestration, release/bootstrap, browser session, and product assertions but never forks Gunicorn or opens the ledger/proof/lock. Every claim reserve/runner-create/runner-discard/activate/refresh/release and readiness observation is a closed request to the outside-parent supervisor, which authenticates the controller PID/start/cgroup/journal state and performs the ledger operation itself. Over one private CLOEXEC socket it sends an authenticated fixed `start-master` request bound to attempt/claim/argv/environment hashes. The supervisor then forks the master through the separate two-phase barrier, remains its direct parent, and returns only `{claim_id,master_pid,pgid,argv_sha256}` after journal `starting`; the controller requests observation of the exact master/two workers/listener; the supervisor performs that observation plus claim activation and returns the hash-bound result before accepting `mark-running`. Gunicorn's closed environment has exactly `ALLOWED_HOSTS,APP_DATABASE_URL,CLINIC_ATTEMPT_ID,CLINIC_DATA_MODE,CLINIC_LEDGER_RPC_SOCKET,CLINIC_PROCESS_CLAIM_ID,COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME,DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE,HOME,LANG,PYTHONTZPATH,SECRET_KEY,TMPDIR` with the previously fixed values; only `APP_DATABASE_URL,SECRET_KEY` are secret and no inherited PATH/Python/proxy/config key survives. The supervisor treats controller EOF, unexpected exit, missed heartbeat, or mismatched request as terminal failure: it journals the controller wait result, terminates the master/domain, completes claim/resource cleanup, and publishes one failure receipt; a controller is never adopted or restarted around a live browser/Gunicorn generation. The supervisor owns a mode-0600 nofollow Unix `SOCK_SEQPACKET` endpoint beneath the journal-bound process-claim root; each controller/master/worker request is canonical, sequence-bound, and authenticated with `SO_PEERCRED`, `/proc` start ticks, and exact descendant-cgroup membership, and responses contain only claim state/hash or a generic failure. Normal controller exit is accepted only after it requests shutdown, the supervisor TERM/KILL-reaps Gunicorn, proves the domain empty and listener absent, releases the process claim through its own ledger authority, closes/removes the RPC endpoint, and seals the journal. If the supervisor dies, both barrier children use `PDEATHSIG=SIGKILL`; workers are contained by the journaled cgroup for successor cleanup. Thus one durable parent owns both child lifecycles and no live supervisor/controller ambiguity is legal.
- Host Gunicorn barrier and SIGKILL-recovery contract: every controller/master fork uses two CLOEXEC socketpairs. The child sets `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG=SIGKILL`, verifies the supervisor PID, calls `setsid`, reports PID/PGID/start ticks, and blocks on the membership socket. The supervisor fsyncs identity before moving that PID, moves it into the exact journal-bound cgroup, verifies membership, fsyncs the member state, then sends the first token. The controller may exec its fixed script immediately after that token. The master instead acknowledges the first token and blocks on the second socket; the supervisor fsyncs `master-exec-authorized` **before** sending the one exec token, after which the child closes all barrier/control FDs and calls exact `os.execve`. EOF, parent change, missing token, identity/membership/journal failure, or supervisor death exits before controller/product or Gunicorn code. A crash after exec authorization is unambiguous: the journal permits a starting/running master and recovery kills the whole domain. Normal failure preserves both child identities/wait results, TERM/KILL-reaps direct children, invokes `cgroup.kill`, waits for `populated 0`, fsyncs remove-intent, removes only the identical empty cgroup inode, fsyncs removed, reconciles/releases the process claim, proves listener/baseline cleanup, and seals one receipt. If the supervisor is SIGKILLed, Todo 14 rerun, every final-wave entrypoint, and ledger reject/close invoke `browser_server_recovery.py` under the stable lock. On the creation boot, recovery accepts only an absent or nonlive recorded supervisor; a live supervisor is allowed only with matching PID/start and a fresh journal heartbeat, otherwise the normal supervisor termination path is requested and bounded before successor cleanup. The successor validates the exact cgroup parent/child inode, uses `cgroup.kill`, waits for `populated 0`, proves controller/master identities absent without non-parent `waitpid`, completes removal idempotently, reconciles only the recorded claim, and seals one failure receipt. A changed boot permits no signal and only stale-ledger recovery may record kernel disappearance. Foreign/replaced domains, unjournaled members, unexplained same-boot absence before removal intent, live listeners after empty-domain proof, or identity drift block.
- Host Gunicorn supervisor closed-state matrix: the mode-0600 journal keys are exactly `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,claim_id,creation_boot_id,recovery_boot_id,state,cgroup_parent_path,cgroup_parent_device,cgroup_parent_inode,cgroup_path,cgroup_device,cgroup_inode,supervisor_pid,supervisor_start_ticks,heartbeat_sequence,heartbeat_at_utc,controller_pid,controller_pgid,controller_start_ticks,controller_barrier_released,controller_wait_exit_code,controller_wait_signal,master_pid,master_pgid,master_start_ticks,master_barrier_released,master_wait_exit_code,master_wait_signal,timed_out,typed_outcome_sha256,failure_receipt_sha256,cleanup_verified,removal_kind,removed_at_utc,updated_at_utc}` and state is exactly `prepared|kill-domain-intent|kill-domain-ready|controller-identity|controller-member|controller-running|master-identity|master-member|master-exec-authorized|starting|running|master-terminal|recovering|kill-domain-remove-intent|kill-domain-removed|success|failure-receipt-written`. `prepared` requires canonical claim/supervisor/cgroup-parent identities, current creation boot, null recovery boot, null child-domain/controller/master/wait/outcome/receipt/removal fields, zero heartbeat, and cleanup false. `kill-domain-intent` adds only the canonical absent child path; `kill-domain-ready` requires its exact device/inode and `populated 0`. `controller-identity` requires a complete controller tuple with barrier false before move; `controller-member` preserves it, proves sole authenticated membership, and remains false; `controller-running` preserves it with barrier true, fresh heartbeat, and null waits. `master-identity` adds a complete master tuple with barrier false before move; `master-member` preserves it with verified membership and false; `master-exec-authorized` sets true in the fsynced journal before the exec token and therefore explicitly permits either blocked-at-second-barrier or executing-but-not-ready observations. `starting` preserves both identities/barriers, requires the master child's token acknowledgement, keeps the process claim reserved and all waits/outcomes null. `running` additionally requires active exact master/two-worker/listener claim and fresh controller heartbeat. `master-terminal` preserves identities and requires exactly one master wait result (`exit_code` xor `signal`); controller wait remains null while the controller is live. Any unexpected controller death records exactly one controller wait result, forces `recovering`, and forbids a replacement controller. `recovering` preserves every nonnull identity, both wait results, timeout flag, and typed outcome while cleanup is false. `kill-domain-remove-intent` requires both children/process group absent, each started child represented by exactly one wait result when the live supervisor was its parent (successor cleanup instead records the authenticated non-parent absence in the typed outcome), `populated 0`, null removal fields, and cleanup false. `kill-domain-removed` preserves the path/device/inode tombstone, requires the path absent, nonnull `removal_kind=rmdir|boot-disappearance` and timestamp, and cleanup false. `success` requires normal controller outcome, intentional master shutdown, released claim, absent listener/domain, cleanup true, null failure receipt, and mode-0400 sealing. `failure-receipt-written` requires cleanup true and a nonnull immutable receipt hash bound to the typed outcome. Timestamps/identities never clear or regress; wait fields obey XOR/nullability and receipt/outcome hashes are immutable. Schema `oneOf` plus transition tests cover every state, both barriers, exec-authorized crash, controller death, supervisor death, wait-result form, timeout, remove-intent/rmdir crash, changed boot, receipt binding, and terminal sealing.
  The committed test-only `ops/testing/totp_test_driver.py` is the only automation bridge. Every invocation takes exact `--mode pending-enrollment|confirmed-next-counter` plus numeric context/code FDs, reads connection/user context from a fresh inherited input FD, uses `clinic_app` in a read-only FORCE-RLS transaction with exact GUCs to select exactly one mode-compatible device, writes exactly six code bytes then EOF to a fresh private output pipe, closes both FDs, and exits. For owner, clinic-admin, and physician enrollment, the already-active runner must first consume that user's password-bearing `*-enroll-start` frame, authenticate, POST the foundation `action=start`, observe the pending device, and return the exact nonsecret `pending-ready` acknowledgement; only then may the controller launch that user's `pending-enrollment` helper and send its code through the matching `*-enroll-confirm` frame. The fixed synthetic persona set is bootstrap owner plus newly provisioned clinic-admin, receptionist, and physician. After owner confirm, each of the three `provision_staff` invocations, in clinic-admin -> receptionist -> physician order, launches its own new `confirmed-next-counter` process; each reads the newly committed owner-device `last_t`, waits at most `device.step+2` seconds for a strictly later counter than the prior consumed command, sends its distinct non-replayed code only to that invocation's fresh Todo 13 PTY, and exits before the next command. After creation, clinic-admin and physician independently complete the same start-ack-helper-confirm sequence; receptionist uses one password-only login and must have no device/helper. The complete harness therefore uses exactly six helper invocations: one owner pending, three owner confirmed-next-counter, and two privileged-staff pending. No pending helper may run before its exact runner acknowledgement; no invocation or pipe is reused; seeds/codes never enter argv, env, Docker inspect, stdout/stderr, files, screenshots, or receipts. Wrong count/order, helper-before-start, missing/foreign/mode-mismatched devices, missing GUCs, non-increasing/reused counters, inherited-FD leakage, short/long output, or logging fails closed.
- Browser secret/suite-channel contract: Todo 14 adds committed `ops/testing/browser_runner_supervisor.py`, `ops/testing/browser_secret_bridge.py`, and contract tests. `browser_server.sh` generates four strong synthetic passwords as mutable in-memory byte arrays, writes each same password through a fresh private FD to its Todo 13 controlling PTY prompt, and gives it to the claimed runner only through a new `pipe2(O_CLOEXEC)` producer; no password is accepted from argv, environment, filesystem, shell text, or inherited stdin. The bridge takes only the numeric FD required by its exact action, `--runner-fd`, and a mode-0400 nonsecret action/claim/sequence/username spec. It reads bounded bytes once and emits one four-byte unsigned big-endian length plus canonical UTF-8 JSON frame. The seven allowed one-shot secret frames, in causal order, are exactly `{action:"owner-enroll-start",claim_id,sequence,username,password}`, `{action:"owner-enroll-confirm",claim_id,sequence,username,totp_code}`, `{action:"admin-enroll-start",claim_id,sequence,username,password}`, `{action:"admin-enroll-confirm",claim_id,sequence,username,totp_code}`, `{action:"receptionist-login",claim_id,sequence,username,password}`, `{action:"physician-enroll-start",claim_id,sequence,username,password}`, and `{action:"physician-enroll-confirm",claim_id,sequence,username,totp_code}`; start/login actions require only a password FD, confirm actions require only a code FD, and a combined password+code frame is invalid. Length is 1..4096; passwords are 20..128 UTF-8 bytes; codes are exactly six ASCII digits; every producer reaches EOF with no trailing byte. The persistent runner accepts only the fixed start -> acknowledgement -> confirm ordering per privileged user: a start frame authenticates and POSTs `action=start`, then returns exact nonsecret `{action,claim_id,sequence,status:"pending-ready"}` without serializing the pending device; the controller launches the matching helper only after that acknowledgement; confirm returns `{action,claim_id,sequence,status:"ok"}` and retains the authenticated context. Receptionist login returns the same claim/sequence-bound `ok` acknowledgement. The runner supervisor solely owns `docker start --attach -i`, forwards frames, closes each FD, and overwrites host/runner buffers. `browser_runner_supervisor.py` exposes on the host only a nonserializable in-process `BrowserSupervisorSession` with exact fields `{claim_id,container_id,profile,origin,runner_pid,control_socket,next_sequence,staging_claim_id,staging_root,publisher_claim_id,publication_authorization_id}`; creation verifies the active claim/service identities, `control_socket` is one private CLOEXEC duplex socket owned by the creating controller, `next_sequence=1`, and every action rechecks the same claim/container/profile/origin/runner PID before incrementing that single sequence. Todo 20 `final_e2e_controller.py` owns exactly one such object continuously from source stage 5 through source stage 8; no shell restart, second supervisor, handle file, socket path, environment token, or serialized context can reacquire it. After three confirmed enrollments plus receptionist login, exactly four authenticated Playwright contexts remain only inside the already-running containerized `browser_session.py` process; the host object retains only identity, sequence, control-channel, and declared staging/publisher identities. Todo 18 adds `ops/testing/browser_suite_contract.py` with frozen `ClinicBrowserContexts(owner,admin,receptionist,physician,origin)` fields holding the four live synchronous Playwright `BrowserContext` objects plus exact origin and the closed suite-ID-to-entrypoint string registry. Only container-resident `browser_session.py` may construct, hold, or pass this object; `browser_runner_supervisor.py`, `browser_server.sh`, and `final_e2e_controller.py` never import a live Playwright handle, receive a context, or cross the process boundary with one. `SuiteSummary` is the frozen exact shape `{schema_version:1,suite,check_ids,artifacts}` where `suite` is the invoked ID, `check_ids` is the sorted unique complete suite-specific allowlist, and `artifacts` is the sorted exact array of `{relative_path,media_kind,size_bytes,sha256}` produced under `/tmp/clinic-browser/evidence/<suite>/`; every UI/runtime suite fixes a nonempty complete artifact allowlist, `media_kind="image/png"|"application/json"`, and success requires the complete check and artifact sets. `BrowserAssertionFailure` carries only one allowlisted `{suite,check_id}` pair, and any other exception is reduced to a generic nonsecret code. The four exact in-process entrypoints all have signature `run_suite(contexts: ClinicBrowserContexts) -> SuiteSummary` and are supplied by their owning downstream todos: Todo 14 adds `tests.browser.test_patient_workflow:run_suite`, Todo 15 adds `tests.browser.test_availability_workflow:run_suite`, Todo 16 adds `tests.browser.test_staff_scheduling_workflow:run_suite`, and Todo 20 adds `tests.browser.test_runtime_https:run_suite`. For Todo 14-16 the one-shot controller accepts only `browser_server.sh suite <patient|availability|scheduling>` and creates, uses, and destroys one session within that same invocation. Todo 20/F3 never invokes a later `browser_server.sh suite` command: its still-running `final_e2e_controller.py` calls `BrowserSupervisorSession.run_suite("runtime-https")` on the exact stage-5 host control object, which only sends the authenticated frame and receives the acknowledgement while the runner-resident process owns the live contexts. Both paths map each accepted suite ID respectively to the exact dispatcher argv `python-call\0<entrypoint>` represented as the two-member vector `["python-call","<entrypoint>"]`; this is a protocol dispatch vector, never an OS command, and no caller-supplied argv exists. It computes `argv_sha256` over each UTF-8 dispatcher-argv member followed by one NUL byte and sends exact claim-authenticated nonsecret frame `{action:"run-suite",claim_id,sequence,suite,argv_sha256}`, where `claim_id` equals the active claim/environment/label and `sequence` is the next positive value in the one session-wide sequence that starts at 1 on owner-enroll-start and advances across every secret action and suite action; F3 therefore reaches runtime-https only after the seven exact secret frames and proves one invariant object/container/PID/control-socket identity plus one contiguous action sequence across enrollment, provisioning, logins, and the runtime suite. The already-running `browser_session.py` process recomputes the allowlisted vector/hash, imports only that exact module/function, verifies the callable signature/source path, and invokes it synchronously in the same Python process with its runner-resident retained `ClinicBrowserContexts`; it never calls `pytest`, `uv`, `subprocess`, `exec`, a shell, or a second interpreter and never serializes a context, storage state, cookie, handle, or credential. A schema-valid `SuiteSummary` whose dirfd/nofollow artifact scan matches every declared size/hash/type maps to exit 0, the typed `BrowserAssertionFailure` maps to exit 1, and any other exception maps to exit 2 after redaction; bounded canonical summaries/errors are captured in memory. The runner returns exact `{action:"run-suite",claim_id,sequence,suite,argv_sha256,status,exit_code,stdout_sha256,stderr_sha256,artifact_manifest_sha256,artifact_count,artifact_bytes}` only after the bounded artifact transfer below; `status="ok"` iff exit 0 plus complete host publication, stdout/stderr fields hash bounded redacted bytes, and the artifact fields bind the canonical sorted `SuiteSummary.artifacts` array. The ordinary `uv run pytest tests/browser/test_*_workflow.py ...` commands test each entrypoint with fixture-owned disposable contexts, while the real `browser_server.sh suite` path directs the existing container process to use only its four already-authenticated runner-resident contexts. Wrong claim, sequence, suite, hash, argv, duplicate/replay, action order, artifact path/type/size/hash/frame/publication, or acknowledgement fails closed. On exit/signal it closes contexts/stdin/pipes/container/profile/XDG-NSS state, recursively removes the runner tmpfs evidence root and host staging directory, and proves no cookie/storage-state/credential/helper/FD residue before runner release. Frame echo, malformed/duplicate/out-of-order action, confirm before pending-ready, helper before acknowledgement, secret in logs/inspect/evidence, reused FD/buffer, context serialization, or container death fails closed and zeroizes/tears down.
- Browser artifact-export contract: Todo 18 owns the runner-side artifact schema, dirfd scanner, framing producer, and fixture validator; Todo 14 owns the host supervisor receiver, filesystem-claim-owned staging, validation, and the lock-governed immutable published-output authorization. The runner retains its read-only root and exact tmpfs-only writable path; no evidence bind/volume, Docker socket, source mount, `docker cp`, archive extraction, or arbitrary container path is allowed. Each suite creates only mode-0600 UID/GID `10001:10001` regular files with link count 1 beneath a freshly created mode-0700 `/tmp/clinic-browser/evidence/<suite>/`; every `relative_path` is NFC UTF-8 POSIX text matching the suite's fixed allowlist beneath `browser/<suite>/`, has no empty/dot/dot-dot component, backslash, control byte, leading slash, or component over 96 bytes, and is opened by dirfd with `O_NOFOLLOW`. PNG files must have a valid PNG signature, exact declared 375/768/1280 or 200%-zoom dimensions, and size 1..8388608 bytes; canonical UTF-8 JSON summaries/manifests are 2..262144 bytes and pass the closed synthetic-persona/redaction/no-direct-ID schema. A suite exports at most 48 files and 67108864 aggregate bytes; any extra/missing file, directory, device, FIFO, socket, symlink, hardlink, sparse-size/hash drift, MIME/signature mismatch, or limit breach fails before transfer. After the in-process entrypoint returns, `browser_session.py` independently dirfd-enumerates that root, validates the exact `SuiteSummary.artifacts`, computes its RFC-8785 manifest SHA-256, and writes over the existing runner stdio protocol one canonical `artifact-manifest` frame `{action,claim_id,sequence,suite,manifest_sha256,count,total_bytes}`, then emits the exact control and raw frames defined by the framing contract below; the final `run-suite` acknowledgement is withheld until host publication succeeds. At session creation the host `BrowserSupervisorSession` binds an active filesystem `staging_claim_id` plus its claim-root-contained `staging_root`, and separately binds a `publisher_claim_id` plus one exact `publication_authorization_id`; it contains no terminal destination path. The staging and publisher IDs may be identical for a todo-local authorization, but F3 requires the staging-only orchestration UUID and distinct persistent final-terminal publisher UUID. Mutable ownership remains limited to the staging claim root; Todo publication uses the stable lock and F3 publication uses `final.lock`. `browser_runner_supervisor.py` accepts no caller path per artifact: it creates a mode-0700 staging directory below the claim root, streams each frame to `openat(O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW,0600)` files while recomputing sizes/hashes, rejects unknown/interleaved/trailing frames, fsyncs files and directories, runs `assert_visual_evidence.py` against the staged canonical manifest, then seals the staged manifest and dispatches through the declared publication route. Todo 14-16 use a todo-local controller call to their bound publisher. F3 instead sends the closed journaled publication request below to its still-alive outer supervisor and blocks without sending `artifact-published` to the runner. The actual publisher nofollow-copies already-validated files into a fresh pending directory beneath the publisher claim root, revalidates the complete allowlist/hashes, file+directory-fsyncs it, and only then no-replace atomically renames that publisher-owned directory into the ledger-fixed publication root; when publisher and staging IDs are identical, the todo-local route may use its already publisher-owned pending directory directly. The publisher fsyncs the destination parent and records hashes only in the publisher claim/journal. The lane staging claim never changes ownership, and neither runner session nor F3 controller opens or receives the terminal destination. For F3 the persistent publisher's destination root is exactly `.omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final/terminal/F3`, and its `f3-artifacts` authorization contains only the fixed `runtime-https` suite PNG/JSON allowlist because F3 invokes only that in-process suite; the runner's all-four available-suite identity is a capability/probe requirement, not a demand to republish Todo 14-16 artifacts. Todo 14-16 use their own fixed attempt-root todo-evidence authorizations. The host then returns an `artifact-published` control acknowledgement bound to the same claim/sequence/suite/manifest hash, permitting the runner to send the final `run-suite` acknowledgement and delete its tmpfs files. A disconnect, timeout, signal, invalid visual/redaction manifest, partial chunk, hash mismatch, existing destination, publication/fsync failure, secret/direct-ID-shaped JSON, or raw stream in evidence deletes only the claim-owned staging and runner-tmpfs data, publishes nothing partial, fails the suite, and enters normal reverse cleanup. Contract tests export multi-chunk PNG/JSON fixtures and causally reject traversal, symlink/hardlink, oversize/count/aggregate, wrong signature/dimensions/hash/order/sequence, truncated/extra frames, arbitrary destination, publication race, and residue.
- F3 supervisor-owned publication RPC: the existing private controller/supervisor control socket admits one additional closed sequence while the same controller retains `BrowserSupervisorSession`. After frame validation and staging fsync, the controller sends canonical request `{op:"publish-request",attempt_id,sha,sequence,staging_claim_id,publisher_claim_id,authorization_id:"f3-artifacts",manifest_sha256,entries_sha256}`; IDs/hashes must equal the active orchestration claim, final-wave journal publisher UUID, frozen authorization, and scanned staging bytes. The supervisor rejects every path or extra key, acquires the stable then final lock order, and fsyncs an exact append-only journal record `{authorization_id,sequence,state,staging_claim_id,publisher_claim_id,manifest_sha256,entries_sha256,publication_sha256,acknowledged_at_utc}` with `state=requested` before import, `imported` after publisher-root pending bytes/fsync, and `published` only after no-replace destination publication, destination-parent fsync, ledger observed update, and re-hash. It returns canonical `{op:"publish-result",attempt_id,sha,sequence,authorization_id,publication_sha256,status:"published"}` only from durable `published`. The controller verifies it, sends the runner its existing `artifact-published` acknowledgement, receives the runner's final `run-suite` success, then sends `{op:"publish-ack",attempt_id,sha,sequence,authorization_id,publication_sha256}`; the supervisor fsyncs `state=controller-acknowledged` with timestamp and only then may stage 8 succeed. Same-sequence byte-identical request/result/ack replay is idempotent; a changed hash/ID/sequence rejects. A crash at `requested|imported` resumes or removes only the exact pending publisher bytes; at `published` replay returns the same result; at `controller-acknowledged` no runner or publisher action repeats. If the controller dies before acknowledgement, the supervisor never reconstructs the browser session: it completes/validates any durable `f3-artifacts` publication, preserves that exact predecessor-valid partial prefix, kills the runner/domain, records a typed failure, seals the failure-ready supervisor journal, and writes only the stable-lock common F3 receipt. `f3-receipt` and `f3-manifest` records are supervisor-internal success-only records and remain absent/unpublished on every failure branch. The terminal destination/capability remains supervisor-only, while the controller has the live session and staging identity; this RPC is the only bridge. Contract tests crash both sides before/after each request/import/publication/result/runner-ack/supervisor-ack fsync and prove no deadlock, duplicate, or false suite success; a crash after durable `f3-artifacts` but before acknowledgement proves that only that partial prefix persists, `f3-receipt|f3-manifest` stay absent, and exactly one common F3 receipt is created after cleanup.

- Browser artifact-framing contract: all control messages use the existing unsigned 32-bit big-endian length followed by 1..65536 bytes of RFC-8785 canonical UTF-8 JSON; no BOM, whitespace variant, duplicate key, noncanonical number, or trailing byte is legal. The manifest frame is exactly `{action:"artifact-manifest",claim_id,sequence,suite,manifest_sha256,count,total_bytes}`. Sorted artifact index `i` then uses exactly `{action:"artifact-begin",claim_id,sequence,suite,index:i,relative_path,media_kind,size_bytes,sha256,chunk_count}`; `index` is contiguous `0..count-1`, and `chunk_count=ceil(size_bytes/1048576)`. Immediately after that control frame the receiver reads exactly `chunk_count` raw frames, each encoded as unsigned 32-bit big-endian length plus payload: every nonfinal payload is exactly 1048576 bytes and the final payload is 1..1048576 bytes, with their sum exactly `size_bytes`. The next frame must be exact control JSON `{action:"artifact-end",claim_id,sequence,suite,index:i,relative_path,size_bytes,sha256}` matching begin and the recomputed stream. After the last end frame, any runner byte before the host response rejects. Host success is exact control JSON `{action:"artifact-published",claim_id,sequence,suite,manifest_sha256,status:"ok"}`; only then may the runner emit the already-defined final `run-suite` acknowledgement, after which EOF or the next expected action frame is required. Zero-length/raw-outside-begin, wrong endian/width, oversized control, wrong chunk count/size/index/order, interleaving, early end, extra chunk/control/trailing byte, or acknowledgement inversion fails closed and publishes nothing.
  `tests/browser/visual_contract.py` supplies shared executable assertions for no horizontal overflow/clipped controls, label/error linkage, visible keyboard focus, touch-target spacing, responsive navigation, and expected screenshot dimensions at 375/768/1280 plus 200% zoom; `ops/testing/assert_visual_evidence.py` validates the JSON screenshot manifest, SHA-256 files, synthetic-persona allowlist, and absence of forbidden identifiers. Cover receptionist native/HTMX flows; physician TOTP + own-agenda scope; every product route's safe TOTP continuation; JS disabled; back/reload; rapid submit; blocked third-party network; and page/request/console errors. Raw Playwright traces/HARs are transient only in a task-owned mode-0700 directory and destroyed before final evidence; retain only allowlisted synthetic screenshots and redacted origin/method/status/console summaries with no path/query/body/header/cookie/TOTP/direct identifier.
- Production matrix: negative runtime/release/browser settings imports, runtime `check --deploy`, owner release plan->migrate->post-check, and real database `verify-full` TLS. Todo 18 pins `docker.io/library/postgres@sha256:4c9405bdf36a7a96c5637acec4b39545681f0d2154a7b1e622890607aad6bf56` (linux/amd64 PostgreSQL 16.14) and uses its asserted OpenSSL 3.5.6 in a task-only materializer to create one mode-0700 CA plus distinct SAN leaves for `phase1a-db.qa.clinic-os.dev` and `phase1a-restore-db.qa.clinic-os.dev`. Database server certificates are 0444 and private keys 0400, numeric `999:999`; the HTTPS web leaf uses 0444/0400 owned by `10001:10001`; all client trust is read-only at exact task paths. Source and restore services run `ssl=on`, `ssl_min_protocol_version=TLSv1.2`, exact cert/key paths, task-network DNS aliases, and readiness that proves `ssl=true`; runtime/release containers use the DNS name plus `sslmode=verify-full`/`sslrootcert`, while a materializer-dependent filesystem claim copies only the public CA certificate to its mode-0700 root as a mode-0444 regular file with exact inode/SHA-256; the host browser process depends on and borrows that file read-only, keeps the DNS `HOST`, and supplies libpq `hostaddr=127.0.0.1` to its reserved loopback port. The export never contains a private key, is verified before settings import and on every refresh, outlives the browser process, and is deleted/released before the materializer in reverse order. Plaintext, wrong CA/SAN/hostname, permissive sslmode, bad mode/owner, missing restored-target TLS, or leaked CA key fails. Preserve search-path options; set `SECURE_PROXY_SSL_HEADER=None` for every direct-process surface with a spoofed `X-Forwarded-Proto` redirect test; configure Sentry `max_request_body_size="never"` plus a `before_send` scrub of request data/query/headers/cookies; prove the exact published wheel hash for `tzdata==2026.3` and empty `zoneinfo.TZPATH`; build/run non-root image; verify `/healthz`, role/schema/migration-aware `/readyz` with connection timeout 1-3 seconds plus 2-second SQL timeout and <=5-second observed failure, self-hosted HTMX/static asset, SIGTERM graceful exit, and mutual rejection of owner runtime/app-role release. Teardown removes both TLS database services, networks, leaves, CA private key, and client trust state through recorded claims.
- CA ownership/readability contract: the materializer stack exclusively owns six uniquely named volumes for six exact logical roles and no borrower uses a volume subpath. Each actual Docker volume name is exactly `<materializer-project>_<materializer-claim-id-without-hyphens>_<logical-role>`; the project and canonical claim UUID make it attempt/claim-unique, the ledger records that actual name plus its full Docker named-volume inspect identity tuple, and no raw logical-role string may be created or mounted. Logical role `phase1a-pki-private` contains only the CA directory/key at `0700/0400 root:root` and is mounted only by the materializer. Logical roles `phase1a-source-db-tls` and `phase1a-restore-db-tls` each contain only `/tls` mode `0700`, `/tls/server.crt` mode `0444`, `/tls/server.key` mode `0400`, and `/tls/pg_hba.conf` mode `0400`, all owned by `999:999`; the two HBA files are byte-identical and share the required SHA-256; Logical role `phase1a-web-tls` contains only `/tls` mode `0700`, `/tls/tls.crt` mode `0444`, and `/tls/tls.key` mode `0400`, all owned by `10001:10001`. Each DB/web claim borrows only its one entire server-leaf/config volume read-only at the exact declared target, and its observed service mount must match the owner volume identity/target/read-only tuple, so no server can traverse or mount the CA-key volume. Logical role `phase1a-materializer-pgdata` is mounted read-write only by the materializer at `/var/lib/postgresql/data`, contains only disposable PostgreSQL-image runtime scratch, and prevents any anonymous image-declared mount. Logical role `phase1a-db-trust` contains only `/trust` mode `0755 root:root` and `/trust/db-ca.pem` mode `0444 root:root`; nonroot UID/GID `10001:10001` runtime/release clients and HTTPS browser-runner instances borrow that entire public volume read-only at `/run/clinic-test-db-trust` through their exact service mount mappings, and activation proves no private-key entry or writable mount. The host export is different: its filesystem claim root is mode 0700 owned by the recorded executor `uid=os.geteuid(),gid=os.getegid()`, `db-ca.pem` is mode 0444 with the same UID/GID and exact device/inode/hash, and the host Gunicorn process claim requires that identical UID/GID. Volume/network/file borrow records and service mappings carry exact owner, network ID or full volume identity plus resource name/path/target/alias/access, mode, UID/GID, inode where host-observable, and hash identities; wrong traversal, identity, access, or content fails. All six resolved actual materializer volumes and the host export remain materializer-owned resources with reverse-topological borrower lifetimes.
- PostgreSQL transport gate: the materializer writes two byte-identical exact mode-0400, owner-`999:999` HBA copies, one in each DB server-leaf/config volume at `/tls/pg_hba.conf`; source and restore service mappings mount their own entire volume read-only at `/run/clinic-test-db-tls` and use `-c hba_file=/run/clinic-test-db-tls/pg_hba.conf`. Its only non-comment rules, in order, are `local all postgres peer`, `local all all reject`, `hostnossl all all 0.0.0.0/0 reject`, `hostnossl all all ::/0 reject`, `hostssl all all 0.0.0.0/0 scram-sha-256`, and `hostssl all all ::/0 scram-sha-256`. The source DB stack exclusively owns network `<source-project>_source`, attaches its DB service with alias `phase1a-db.qa.clinic-os.dev`, and the restore stack exclusively owns `<restore-project>_restore` with alias `phase1a-restore-db.qa.clinic-os.dev`; release/web/browser-image consumers declare attach-only borrows of the matching network ID and exact service aliases, never an undeclared default network. PostgreSQL starts with `listen_addresses=*`; initialization may use only the first peer rule as OS/database user `postgres`, while every owner/app/super data connection uses task-network TCP TLS and password authentication. Catalog/file checks prove both materialized HBA hashes are equal, each server loaded its own exact path, and both parsed orders are exact. A real `sslmode=disable` TCP attempt is rejected at HBA for each database; `verify-full` succeeds, and settings separately reject `require` or any weaker client mode.
- Recovery matrix: stop writers and require a task-owned source containing exactly one synthetic organization plus the system-chain bootstrap rows. The exact included `clinic_app` relations are table data for `identity_organization`, `identity_user`, `identity_clinic`, `identity_userclinicrole`, `otp_totp_totpdevice`, `intake_patient`, `intake_patientclinicenrollment`, `scheduling_availabilityblock`, `scheduling_appointment`, and `audit_event`, plus sequence sets for `otp_totp_totpdevice_id_seq` and `audit_event_seq_seq`. Source guards require `identity_user_groups`, `identity_user_user_permissions`, `otp_static_staticdevice`, `otp_static_statictoken`, and `tenancy_tenantprobe` empty. Target-owned framework state (`auth_*`, `django_admin_log`, `django_content_type`, `django_migrations`, `django_session`, OTP-static tables, their sequences, and identity M2M tables) is explicitly excluded and recreated only by owner migrations. Use the disposable project's PostgreSQL 16 source/target container binaries and isolated `clinic_super`; dump with `pg_dump --data-only --format=custom --column-inserts --strict-names` plus one exact schema-qualified `--table` per included table/sequence, normalize `pg_restore --list`, and require exact `TABLE DATA`/`SEQUENCE SET` equality before mutation. Bootstrap/migrate a new isolated target at the same SHA as `clinic_owner`, then restore with exact `--schema=clinic_app`/`--table` selectors, `--strict-names --data-only --no-owner --no-acl --single-transaction --exit-on-error`. Prove all schema/function ownership, sequence continuation, ACL/RLS posture, audit chains, and application behavior as owner/app. Measure elapsed recovery time, delete task artifacts and verify absence, and label evidence `logical restore only; provider PITR not verified; encrypted provider backup not verified`.
- Evidence: `<todo_evidence_root>/task-<N>-clinic-os-phase-1a-staff-scheduling.json`; allowlisted screenshots and redacted summaries under `<todo_evidence_root>/task-<N>-clinic-os-phase-1a-staff-scheduling/`. Every receipt is closed schema version 1 with exact keys `schema_version,todo,foundation_sha,parent_sha,primary_commit_sha,validator_sha256,tdd_entries,tests_after_entries,acceptance,resources,cleanup`; each `tdd_entries[]` is `{test_paths,test_source_sha256,red,green}` and red/green are `{argv,started_at_utc,ended_at_utc,exit_code,classification,expected_failed_nodeids,observed_failed_nodeids,stdout_sha256,stderr_sha256}`. Red requires exit 1, `classification=expected-assertion-failure`, identical nonempty expected/observed node-ID sets, no error/collection/timeout, and `test_source_sha256` equal to the later green entry; green uses the same argv/node IDs and exit 0 after implementation. `tests_after_entries` is allowed only with `classification=visual-polish-no-behavior` and names the already-green HTTP contract. `primary_commit_sha` must have `parent_sha` as its parent and contain the test paths. Todo 1 owns immutable closed schemas plus `ops/testing/publish_todo_receipt.py`; after the primary commit exists, each todo writes mode-0600 candidate bytes only beneath its active filesystem-claim staging root and invokes exact `publish_todo_receipt.py --staged <claim-file> --destination <todo_evidence_root>/task-<N>-clinic-os-phase-1a-staff-scheduling.json --commit <primary_commit_sha>`. The tool nofollow-opens and fully validates schema, Git parent/test/source hashes, red/green chronology/node IDs, acceptance/resource/cleanup identities, redaction, destination allowlist, and its own clean-Git SHA-256; only a green validation may hold the stable lock and no-replace/fsync publish mode-0400 destination bytes, while any invalid staging is claim-cleaned and leaves the immutable destination absent. `validator_sha256` is that Todo-1 module hash, later commits may not modify it, and Todo 19's aggregate validator must import/reuse the identical module/hash. Todo 19 extends the same stage-before-publish primitive to its closed supplemental review-fix receipts, then replays Git/hash/chronology constraints for all twenty primary receipts plus every supplemental receipt before final freeze. Acceptance/resources/cleanup record exact argv/exit/redacted output hashes, typed claim IDs, and absence proof. Never persist raw trace/HAR, raw stdout/stderr, secrets, cookies, TOTP seeds/codes, direct patient/enrollment IDs, request bodies, or natural-person data.

## Execution strategy
### Parallel execution waves
> Target 5-8 todos per wave. Fewer than 3 (except the final) means you under-split.

- **Preflight:** create the feature worktree/branch from exact foundation SHA, confirm both source worktrees and protected refs are clean/unchanged, inventory unrelated dirty paths and containers, and create Todo 1's exact machine-readable evidence/isolated-resource ledger before claiming any resource. The main worktree's untracked `initial_plan_en.md` and `initialreport.md` remain planning provenance only: do not copy, stage, or depend on them; this plan's fixed contracts are the execution authority. Do not cherry-pick or implement on `main`.
- **Wave 1 - boundaries and schema (Todos 1-6):** isolation harness, trusted actor/RBAC/ACL/resolver invariant, clinic timezone, patient/enrollment schema, availability schema, and versioned audit vocabulary. Todo 3 follows Todo 2 because both own the identity migration leaf; Todos 4 and 6 may proceed independently after Todo 1; Todo 5 follows Todo 3. Each migration owner rebases only through normal integration and never edits a prior migration.
- **Wave 2 - domain workflow (Todos 7-13):** appointment schema, patient services, availability services, appointment creation, appointment transitions, agenda queries, and owner-controlled provisioning. Todo 7 waits for patient+availability schema; Todo 9 also waits for Todo 7 because retirement locks appointment rows; command/query tasks then run at maximum safe parallelism by file ownership.
- **Wave 3 - delivery and safeguards (Todos 14-20):** production settings land first. After all Phase 1A tables and Todo 13's sole guarded bootstrap path exist, Todo 18 independently lands the self-contained runtime/static/readiness artifact and amd64 browser runner; Todo 14 then consumes both. Patient UI -> availability UI -> appointment/agenda UI are serialized because they share navigation/styles. The patient task intentionally has no Book link until Todo 16 adds the matching POST-only booking route. Todo 19 lands CI plus the concrete isolated F1/F2 review-gate runner after the UI surfaces stabilize; Todo 20 adds recovery and the final production-HTTPS/recovery orchestrator. Exact-SHA external reviews/E2E run only after Todo 20 is committed in F1-F3, avoiding a pre-commit SHA cycle.
- **Final verification:** after all 20 primary commits, their machine-bound receipts, and the authenticated cross-attempt globally gap-free supplemental review-fix lineage are integrated, Todo 19 creates or locked-resumes the current attempt's task control root, lock, phase journal, terminal namespace, and immutable approved-plan/SHA/tree/source/diff/receipt inputs; Todo 1's lstat/Merkle baseline already records every unrelated shared-evidence sibling and each lane verifies it without mutation. F1-F3 and F4 source precheck run under shared locks and claim-owned scratch. After join, any non-green F1-F3 or F4 source precheck goes directly to its authenticated rejection path and skips pre-F4/F4/final. Only exact F1-F3 approvals plus `APPROVE-PRECHECK` let the exclusive phase-recovery freezer validate-or-create `pre-f4.json` for exactly `PRE_F4`; only freezer success lets the staged decision gate evaluate F4. The outside-parent controller validates that handoff and the persistent publisher alone creates canonical `F4-final.txt`; only a published literal `APPROVE` then lets the separately supervised freezer create-or-validate `final.json` for exactly `FINAL` with the canonical approval array/hash, after which the controller performs the third recursive equality check before releasing the lock. The ledger remains open and dependents release in reverse order. Rejection tears down, revalidates the unrelated baseline, records the immutable reason/retry-kind spec, closes the ledger, and archive-rolls the validated absent-or-partial-or-complete control root, complete attempt root, and closed ledger into `.omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-rejected/<attempt_id>/<sha>/` with exact attempt/seed/spec/ledger hashes, the final-lineage hash when present, and the control hash when present in the immutable tombstone; a null control hash is legal only for an authenticated pre-root ORCH rejection at `ORCH-input-freeze`. No archived receipt is edited or “marked.” Mandatory after-archive snapshot inventories that bundle and carries forward its authenticated receipt sources into a new lineage seed; evidence-only failures rebuild the whole wave at the identical SHA with no commit/receipt, while source-fix failures first add narrow Lore fixes and supplemental receipts and then rebuild at the new SHA. Success retains the exact FINAL control tree with an open, zero-claim baseline-clean ledger while the exact SHA/tree/four approvals/canonical `approvals_sha256`/inventory are presented for user acceptance. An in-scope requested repair follows the same USER rejection/archive/lineage path; explicit acceptance triggers one refreshed final close/receipt and never rolls over.

### File ownership guard

- Identity owners: Todo 2 owns current actor, shared username/email canonicalizers, authentication-backend username normalization, runtime ACLs, and the physician resolver migration; Todo 3 then owns the timezone migration; Todo 13 owns owner-lifecycle commands/base plus the management-TOTP helper and tests. Intake owners: `apps/intake/**`; scheduling schema/service/UI owners: separate model/migration, service, and form/view/template slices; audit owner: Python canonicalization plus a new reversible v2 SQL-function migration; ops owner: settings/image/Make/CI/docs.
- Shared files (`config/urls.py`, `config/settings/*`, `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `templates/base.html`, common CSS, `Makefile`, CI, schema-policy/module-boundary tests) are integrated by one designated owner in dependency order. Workers must report conflicts rather than overwrite concurrent changes.
- Python implementation uses repository-enforced strict mypy/Ruff/pytest gates: strict types, typed domain errors, no `Any` escape, no `# type: ignore`, no oversized pure-code module above 250 LOC, parse-don't-validate boundaries, and structured logs without patient data.

The input manifest binds the launcher path/lstat/checksum file paths, contents, and hashes, the exact launcher lstat, resolved-target realpath/hash, a complete canonical nofollow manifest of every entry below `<worktree>/.venv` with its aggregate SHA-256, the approved uv launcher/symlink-chain/resolved regular path/lstat/hash, the approved Codex launcher/symlink-chain/resolved static-linux-amd64-ELF path/lstat/hash plus no-interpreter/no-needed proof, exact canonical nofollow identities/paths for the ledger, stable lock, attempt root, common F3 failure receipt, and final control/evidence roots, plus the complete nonnull ledger `execution_host_preflight` object, absolute proof path, proof bytes/SHA/file identity, creation boot ID, and delegated-parent path/device/inode. Immediately before publication it nofollow-revalidates the proof and ledger binding, requires the current boot/parent/control identities unchanged, then uses the exact final-input capability-probe journal to complete one uniquely named child create/parent-verified identity-fsync/move/membership-fsync/release/`cgroup.kill`/`populated 0`/remove-intent/`rmdir`/removed probe beneath only that parent; `inputs.json` binds the proof plus the immutable Todo-1 kickoff and final-input probe paths/hashes, and any drift, unexplained absence, incomplete journal, or extra probe artifact fails closed. The final-wave journal, `input-sidecar` authorization, and PRE_F4/FINAL inventories bind `inputs.sha256`; `inputs.json` intentionally cannot hash the sidecar that hashes itself. It also contains exact SHA/tree, exact `final_required_browser_suites=["availability","patient","runtime-https","scheduling"]` plus the SHA-256 of tracked LF-terminated Todo-20 `ops/testing/final-required-browser-suites.txt`, and separately the exact three-suite CI-file hash proving host-HTTP CI did not acquire `runtime-https`, both content-addressed image IDs and closed image contracts, the runner filesystem contract, both candidate-envelope paths/hashes, both exact publication-history paths/hashes and authorization/predecessor digests, and both absent publisher claim UUIDs, approved-plan/diff/source/lineage hashes, exactly 20 resolved primary-receipt hashes plus the globally gap-free supplemental chain, and `f3_runtime_artifact_paths` equal only the frozen `runtime-https` PNG/JSON allowlist. Its closed atomic authorization set is exactly the IDs defined by schema v2; `PRE_F4` lists `inputs.json`, `inputs.sha256`, `F3-launcher.path`, `F3-launcher.lstat`, `F3-launcher.sha256`, every required F1/F2/F3 success authorization, and `F4-pre.txt`; `FINAL` is `PRE_F4` plus an approving `F4-final.txt`. Every evidence/reviewer/decision lane child writes only inside its claim-owned staging root; its durable controller holds shared `final.lock` only while the persistent publisher atomically creates that lane's declared terminal path. The separately supervised final freezer is not a lane decision child: it is the sole narrow control-artifact writer, receives only the fixed `final.json` destination after publisher-confirmed approval, and can create no terminal path, decision, receipt, or publisher state. Lane interruption never resumes in place and instead creates its authenticated failure receipt and takes the evidence-only-or-source-fix rejection path. The `final` form is the sole two-stage controller form, with ordered stages `f4-decision -> final-freeze`: its gate child stages only canonical decision/outcome bytes, the controller asks the persistent publisher to publish `f4-final`, and only an already-published approving decision permits the separately supervised freezer to create-or-validate control-root `final.json`.
  Final-terminal publisher journal matrix: in `terminal_publisher_state=unbound`, the claim ID, all four reservation fields, and every release field are null, no publisher ledger claim or terminal destination exists, and no authorization may publish. The only transition is `id-bound`, which allocates one canonical UUID plus reservation timestamp, binds the exact spec and pre/post ledger hashes, and fsyncs that write-ahead intent before reservation. In `id-bound`, release fields remain null and exactly two ledger shapes are legal: the bound pre-reservation hash with publisher absent, or the bound post-reservation hash with the same UUID present as the exact reserved claim with empty observations/authorizations and no claim root, staging, or output. Recovery from pre performs the single bound replacement; recovery from post performs no ledger write; both then fsync `reserved`. In `reserved`, the ledger must equal the reservation post-hash and that UUID exists as the reserved `final-terminal-publisher` claim with every authorization unpublished/empty; no terminal file exists, and only exact ordinary activation or the rejection-only all-empty `reserved -> release-intent` transition is legal. In `active`, the same claim is active, completed authorizations are immutable, pending ones empty, release fields null, and only predecessor-satisfied publication/recovery may advance. `release-intent` is reachable from `active` only after exact approved-chain or rejection-prefix validation, or directly from `reserved` only for authenticated rejection with absent root/staging, an all-empty authorization vector, absent destinations, and the finalized receipt aggregate; it binds the release kind/context/boot/basis/authorization-state plus complete pre/post ledger hashes before claim removal, and no publication or authorization mutation is legal. In `released`, the ledger contains no publisher claim, the exact post-release hash validates, every intent field remains unchanged, `released_at_utc` is nonnull, either the complete approved chain or exact rejection partial prefix is immutable, and no terminal creation, reservation, or regression is legal. Recovery from release intent accepts only pre-hash plus exact publisher present or post-hash plus publisher absent. `phase=initializing` permits `unbound|id-bound|reserved|active` and, only during authenticated rejection recovery, `release-intent|released`; nonterminal `inputs-frozen|lanes-complete|pre-f4-frozen|f4-created` requires `active`; `f4-rejected|rejected|final-frozen` permits `active` only before release validation, `release-intent` only during the bound removal replay, and requires `released` afterward. `phase=final-frozen` with publisher `active|release-intent|released` is a recoverable approved prefix: on the creation boot the final-wave controller may finish it, but after a boot change only `reconcile --terminal-final` may advance it. Terminal artifact creation requires `released`, the exact post-release ledger hash, `claims=[]`, and zero task resources. Journal `oneOf` fixtures cover every legal phase/state/nullability/hash combination; kill fixtures bracket id-bound journal temp/fsync/replace/parent-fsync, ledger temp/fsync/replace/parent-fsync, reserved journal temp/fsync/replace/parent-fsync, and the reserved-to-active ledger/journal pair on both same- and changed-boot recovery. They accept only `id-bound+pre/absent`, `id-bound+post/exact-reserved`, `reserved+post/exact-reserved`, and the exact `reserved-journal+active-ledger` activation prefix, converging to one UUID/claim, and reject unbound+claim, reserved+pre/absent, id-bound+active, wrong UUID/spec/timestamp/hash, extra mutation or publisher, root/staging/output existence, nonempty authorization, null-ID after binding, nonnull-ID while unbound, any early/non-null release field, a named/replaced UUID, a state skip, authorization drift, publication after intent, a release pre/post mismatch, or publication by a lane claim. An F1/F2/F3/ORCH failure advances to `rejected` only after its sealed failure-ready journal and authenticated common receipt validate, its exact rejection prefix is every already-published successful-lane authorization plus optional `f3-artifacts` only when durable before F3 failure, every failed/unrun/successor authorization is unpublished/empty, and the journaled release completes; semantic F4 rejection uses `f4-rejected` under the same rule.
  Final-wave outer-controller contract: `final_wave_controller.py` accepts only four closed forms: `inputs --sha <40-hex> --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final --output .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final/terminal/inputs.json`; `scope-pre --sha <40-hex> --inputs .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final/terminal/inputs.json --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final --output .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final/terminal/F4-pre.txt`; `pre-f4 --sha <40-hex> --inputs <exact-inputs> --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final --namespace .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final/terminal --output .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final/pre-f4.json`; and `final` with the exact fixed non-destination arguments listed in F4; it rejects caller-supplied `--evidence|--final-inventory`, derives both canonical destinations internally, and gives neither destination to the decision child. Unknown flags, paths, aliases, trailing argv, or a second concurrent form fail before spawn. It constructs rather than accepts child argv and maps only to `freeze_final_wave_inputs.py`, `scope_gate.sh`, `freeze_final_wave_outputs.py`, `final_artifact_gate.sh`, or the final form's second child `freeze_final_wave_final.py`, with closed per-stage deadlines. The decision child receives only its claim-root staging destinations; the freezer receives only the fixed control-root `final.json` destination after publisher-confirmed approval. Its sole mutable recovery record is canonical mode-0600 `<attempt_root>/final-wave-controller.json` under the stable/final lock order, with exact keys `{schema_version,attempt_id,sha,form,creation_boot_id,recovery_boot_id,state,stage,completed_stages,final_gate_staging_claim_id,final_gate_staging_state,staged_f4_sha256,staged_outcome_sha256,controller_lease_path,controller_lease_identity,controller_lease_state,controller_owners,child_argv_sha256,started_at_utc,child_pid,child_pgid,child_start_ticks,child_barrier_released,termination_kind,boot_disappearance_sha256,wait_exit_code,wait_signal,timed_out,typed_outcome_sha256,cleanup_verified,updated_at_utc}` and `state="prepared|child-barrier|child-running|child-terminal|between-stages|recovering|failure-ready|success"`. `termination_kind` is null before a terminal observation, `wait-result` for a normal child/controller outcome, and `boot-disappearance` only when stale-boot recovery authenticates different creation/current boots; `boot_disappearance_sha256` is null except in that last case, where it hashes the exact prior/current boot observations and wait exit/signal/typed-outcome fields are null with `timed_out=false`. For `inputs|scope-pre|pre-f4`, `stage|completed_stages|final_gate_staging_claim_id|final_gate_staging_state|staged_f4_sha256|staged_outcome_sha256` are null or empty by the closed schema. For `final`, `stage` is `f4-decision|final-freeze`, `completed_stages` is the ordered immutable prefix, `final_gate_staging_claim_id` is one canonical UUID, and staging state is `unreserved|reserved|active|released`; the claim has `purpose="f4-final-staging"` and `published_outputs=[]`. Its gate writes literal `F4-final.txt` first and then directory-fsyncs mode-0400 `F4-outcome.json` with exact keys `{schema_version:1,attempt_id,sha,inputs_sha256,pre_f4_sha256,decision,decision_relative_path:"staging/F4-final.txt",decision_sha256}`; both valid semantic decisions exit 0. The controller accepts no semantic result until both hashes are nonnull, validates the complete staging directory, and uses `between-stages` with no live child between decision publication and optional final freeze. Before any journal or child, the controller stable-lock creates-or-nofollow-opens the one zero-length mode-0600 no-replace `<attempt_root>/final-wave-controller.lease`, fsyncs its fixed `{device,inode,mode,uid,gid,link_count}` identity, and acquires `flock(LOCK_EX|LOCK_NB)` on that exact inode. `controller_lease_path` and `controller_lease_identity` never change; `controller_lease_state=held|released`; and `controller_owners` is an append-only array of exact `{sequence,boot_id,pid,start_ticks,acquired_at_utc,relinquished_at_utc,relinquish_kind}` records, where sequence starts at 1, `boot_id` is the current boot at acquisition, only the last record may have null relinquishment fields while held, and `relinquish_kind=clean-release|stale-owner-recovery|changed-boot-recovery` when nonnull. If only the authenticated lease exists because death occurred before first journal publication, a successor may initialize `prepared` only after acquiring that inode and proving the form's output/journal/resource prefix otherwise absent; lock contention proves the pre-journal controller still live. `prepared` requires the closed form/argv hash, `creation_boot_id` equal the current boot, `recovery_boot_id=null`, lease held by the last complete same-boot controller PID/start identity, and null child/wait/outcome fields; `child-barrier` requires complete PID/PGID/start identity with `child_barrier_released=false`; `child-running` requires the same identity and true; `child-terminal` is creation-boot-only and requires `termination_kind=wait-result`, exactly one exit-code-or-signal outcome, boolean timeout, null boot-disappearance hash, and nullable typed hash. `success` preserves that wait result; `recovering|failure-ready` preserve it for normal recovery; the sole changed-boot preservation exception is a publisher-canonical F4 REJECT whose child `exit_code=0`, staged typed outcome, and published authorization were all durable before reboot. Every other changed-boot interruption uses the exclusive boot-disappearance/null-wait matrix. All variants require `cleanup_verified=true` only after absence/baseline proof. The child sets PDEATHSIG, verifies parent, creates a session, signals ready, and blocks while the controller journals/fsyncs identity before release. A live controller directly waits/reaps and handles `HUP|INT|TERM` by TERM->bounded wait->KILL/reap. On a classified failure it first recovers any F3 journal/kill domain, restores claims/resources/baseline, closes/releases its lease, seals and re-hashes `failure-ready`, and only then writes the common receipt. `scope-pre` maps to lane F4 stage `F4-precheck`; a semantic F4 decision becomes canonical only when the persistent publisher records the matching published `f4-final` authorization. Canonical REJECT always maps to lane F4 stage `F4-final` and product-failure/source-fix after staging cleanup, even across controller death or boot change. Missing/malformed staging, child failure, or any failure before F4 publication maps to `ORCH-final-gate` with neither artifact. A mechanical publisher/final-freezer failure after canonical APPROVE also maps to `ORCH-final-gate`, retaining the approving F4 prefix and no `final.json`; canonical REJECT is never relabeled ORCH. `inputs|pre-f4` map to `ORCH-input-freeze|ORCH-pre-f4-freeze`. If the controller is SIGKILLed on its creation boot, every later form or `reject` first nofollow-opens the exact lease and attempts the nonblocking lock: lock contention plus matching recorded same-boot PID/start proves a live owner and rejects the duplicate; an acquired lock is legal only when the last recorded same-boot owner PID/start is absent, atomically closes that owner with `stale-owner-recovery`, appends the successor current-boot PID/start as the next owner, and fsyncs the journal before recovery. It then authenticates the same-boot child PID/PGID/start ticks, signals TERM/KILL, observes disappearance without non-parent `waitpid`, restores the baseline, and advances `recovering` to sealed `failure-ready` plus its later common receipt or validated success. A free lease with a still-live same-boot recorded owner, changed inode, malformed owner history, missing journal beside noninitial state, or takeover without the fsynced append fails closed. This ordinary takeover is same-boot only. On a boot mismatch every controller form and `reject` refuses, `recovery_boot_id` remains null, and no recorded PID/PGID is signaled. The sole approved exception is `reconcile --terminal-final` consuming the exact post-FINAL `form=final` success-compatible prefix defined above; it may only validate/finish success and remove the record, never convert it to a receipt. Every other prefix is handled only by `reconcile --stale-boot`, which after binding this exact journal/lease into its immutable `controller_recoveries` list may acquire the free fixed lease inode, set `recovery_boot_id` to the current boot, close the prior owner with `changed-boot-recovery`, append/fsync its current recovery owner, prove all prior-boot processes absent, reconcile only outer-journal-authorized claims/resources, and restore the baseline. For the exact publisher-canonical REJECT prefix it preserves the durable `termination_kind=wait-result`, exit 0, typed semantic outcome, and F4 hash, adds `boot-changed` only as a lower-precedence observed cleanup cause, releases the recovery owner/lease, seals `failure-ready`, and creates the lane-F4 product-failure receipt. Every other prefix sets `termination_kind=boot-disappearance` with the authenticated boot hash and null wait/outcome fields before release/seal and creates the mapped infrastructure receipt. Both complete before publisher release or ledger boot update. An inputs child lost before the lineage checkpoint reruns the closed resolver and must publish/revalidate the identical checkpoint before a fallback receipt. GNU `timeout` is only a longer guard. After failure cleanup and the typed outcome are durable, `failure-ready` closes the current owner with `clean-release`, fsyncs `controller_lease_state=released`, seals and re-hashes the record mode 0400, and unlocks; no later form may start in that rejected attempt. Only after that immutable journal hash exists does the live controller or journal-bound recovery create-or-byte-validate the common receipt that references it. Success writes/fsyncs `success`, validates the child output/journal transition, closes the current owner with `clean-release`, fsyncs `controller_lease_state=released`, unlocks only afterward, then removes only this record and fsyncs its parent; the fixed lease inode remains for the next form, whose new record reacquires it and starts its form-local owner sequence at 1, and a crash before removal idempotently validates success. Tests cover every state/nullability/lease-owner transition, duplicate live invocation, stale-owner takeover, crash before/after lease creation/lock/owner append/release, and SIGKILL child/controller boundary for all four forms, proving one direct reap or authenticated disappearance, one post-cleanup receipt, or success—never an orphan, child-authored receipt, undiscoverable record, or timeout-only classification. Final-form fixtures additionally kill at every staged decision/outcome fsync, publisher destination/authorization fsync, `between-stages|f4-created` update, freezer publication, and receipt boundary; reject any gate argv containing a terminal/control destination or publisher capability, any destination without published authorization, any staging member in PRE_F4/FINAL, and any public `--evidence|--final-inventory` flag.

If death occurs after lease creation/lock but before first journal publication, a successor may initialize only after acquiring the same inode and proving the lane journal, roots, claims, and outputs absent; lock contention proves the pre-journal owner live. After the first held owner is fsynced, the controller records immutable `creation_boot_id` equal to the current boot, null `recovery_boot_id`, and allocates/fsyncs the immutable filesystem UUID fields in `prepared`; it then journals `filesystem-reserved` after exact reservations and `filesystem-active` only after authenticated root/tool materialization. On success only, it requests terminal publication through the journal-bound publisher UUID whose `purpose="final-terminal-publisher"`; on failure it publishes no terminal authorization, seals its immutable `failure-ready` journal, and only then writes the exact common F1/F2 receipt directly beneath `<failure_receipt_root>` under the stable lock. A free lease with a live same-boot recorded owner, changed inode, malformed/gapped owner history, or unjournaled takeover fails closed. On a boot mismatch every ordinary lane controller and `reject` refuses, leaves `recovery_boot_id=null`, and never signals a recorded PID/PGID. Only `reconcile --stale-boot`, after binding the exact journal/lease into its immutable `controller_recoveries` list, may acquire the free fixed lease inode, set `recovery_boot_id` to the current boot, close the prior owner with `changed-boot-recovery`, append/fsync its current recovery owner, prove every prior-boot process absent, reconcile only the outer-journal action plan, restore the baseline, set `termination_kind=boot-disappearance` with its authenticated boot hash and null wait/outcome fields, release the recovery owner/lease, seal `failure-ready`, and then create-or-validate the common lane receipt before publisher release or ledger boot update. F1 constructs the fixed `run_review_gate.sh F1 "$SHA" <review-workspace-root>/staging/F1-verdict.json 1800` argv. F2 first constructs the input-authenticated absolute-uv `sync` argv as its `environment-sync` child under a distinct process claim, then constructs `f2_gate.sh` with `<review-workspace-root>/staging/F2-prerequisites.json`, and only after that schema-valid green staged receipt constructs `run_review_gate.sh F2 "$SHA" <review-workspace-root>/staging/F2-verdict.json 1800`; neither child receives a terminal/control-root destination, and the controller alone converts the validated staged outputs into exact final-terminal publications; no shell `finally`, trap, GNU `timeout`, reviewer process, or child writes a common receipt. On success the controller validates the exact lane outputs and their persistent-publisher authorization states, removes/releases every journal-bound filesystem claim, sets `filesystem_cleanup_verified=true`, closes the last controller owner with `clean-release`, fsyncs `controller_lease_state=released` and `success`, unlocks only afterward, changes its journal to mode 0400, and leaves the fixed lease plus journal as recovery evidence. On failure it instead proves the lane's terminal authorizations remain unpublished/empty, performs the same child/filesystem cleanup, closes/releases its lease owner, sets `terminal_outputs_sha256=null`, fsyncs `failure-ready`, seals and re-hashes the journal mode 0400, and only then creates-or-byte-validates the stable-lock common receipt whose `journal_sha256` binds those final bytes; the publisher merely validates that receipt when the rejection path releases its own claim and never imports it. Contract tests exercise duplicate live invocation, stale-owner takeover, crash before/after lease create/lock/owner append/release, and SIGKILL child and controller before/after every journal/fsync/release/wait/publication or stable-receipt boundary for both lanes and prove one authenticated terminal journal, no orphan, no claim/resource leak, and no duplicate/missing terminal output.
  F1/F2 controller closed-state matrix: `controller_lease_state=held` in every nonterminal state and requires the exact fixed lease identity plus one append-only contiguous owner history whose last record is the only unrelinquished record; `success|failure-ready` require that last record closed with `clean-release` and `controller_lease_state=released`. `creation_boot_id` is immutable and equals the boot of the first owner; `recovery_boot_id=null` on its creation boot and equals exactly one later current boot only under outer stale-boot recovery. A same-boot stale-owner recovery closes exactly the former last record with `stale-owner-recovery` and appends exactly one held successor before any other field changes; changed-boot recovery closes it with `changed-boot-recovery`, never signals its PID/PGID, and appends only the outer reconciler owner. `filesystem_state` is exactly `unreserved|reserved|active|released`. `review_workspace_claim_id` is a canonical UUID in every state; `private_environment_claim_id` is null for F1 and one canonical UUID in every F2 state. Both are allocated and fsynced in `prepared` before ledger reservation and never change. `stage` is the next/current/last literal stage; `completed_stages` is append-only and preserves `{stage,process_claim_id,child_pid,child_pgid,child_start_ticks,barrier_released,argv_sha256,exit_code,signal,timed_out,typed_outcome_sha256}` for every terminal child before current-child fields clear. `prepared` requires current `creation_boot_id`, null `recovery_boot_id`, and the controller lease held by the complete current-boot PID/start owner, `filesystem_state="unreserved"`, null tool/tree hashes, empty completed stages, null current claim/child/wait/outcome/terminal-output fields, both cleanup flags false, and no matching filesystem claim/root. `filesystem-reserved` requires the exact review claim reserved and, for F2, the exact private-environment claim reserved, both roots absent/empty, `filesystem_state="reserved"`, and all child/tool/tree fields null. `filesystem-active` requires those claims active, the authenticated review root plus Codex binary present, F2's private dirs/uv shim present when applicable, `filesystem_state="active"`, nonnull input-matching `codex_tool_sha256` for both lanes, `uv_tool_sha256` nonnull only for F2 and null for F1, `private_tree_sha256` still null until F2 sync and always null for F1, no child, and cleanup false. A crash in any of these states may only complete or remove the exact journaled UUID/root; it cannot allocate a replacement. `child-barrier` requires one newly reserved canonical-UUID process claim, complete child identity/argv, null wait/outcome, barrier false, and `child_cleanup_verified=false`; `child-running` preserves those values, requires the process claim active and barrier true, and keeps wait/outcome null. On the creation boot, `child-terminal|recovering` preserve all current identities and require `termination_kind=wait-result`, null boot-disappearance hash, exactly one wait result (`exit_code` xor `signal`, with timeout legal only on the closed TERM/KILL path), nonnull typed outcome, and child cleanup false. Changed-boot `recovering` instead sets `termination_kind=boot-disappearance`, the authenticated boot hash, null wait/typed outcome, and false timeout before cleanup; it never invents a completed stage. After authenticated disappearance, process-claim release, and baseline proof, the controller appends the completed record, sets child cleanup true, then clears current child/process/wait/outcome fields. Each next stage allocates a fresh process UUID and transitions to `child-barrier`; no earlier UUID/history is overwritten. `environment-sync` depends on the private-environment claim; `prerequisites` depends on that claim plus the review-workspace claim; each F1/F2 `review` process depends on its review-workspace claim and, for F2, its private-environment claim.
