Todo 18 gives source alias `phase1a-db.qa.clinic-os.dev`; restore uses distinct `phase1a-restore-db.qa.clinic-os.dev`; both enforce the six-rule HBA and verify-full connections. The controller rechecks before any source or restored service claim that the input-frozen application image ID/contract and browser-runner image ID/contract both equal the current SHA/tree and embedded manifests; the application contract has an empty suite set, the runner contract has exactly `available_suite_ids=["availability","patient","runtime-https","scheduling"]`, and every source/restored release/web/TLS service uses that one application image ID. The single source-only `container-https` runner and its invariant `BrowserSupervisorSession` initialize and verify the Chromium XDG-discovered `/tmp/clinic-browser/xdg/pki/nssdb`, import only the mounted CA, and later accepts only the claim/sequence/argv-hash authenticated allowlisted `runtime-https` in-process entrypoint, invoked in that same runner process with its retained `ClinicBrowserContexts` and no context serialization. `final_e2e_controller.py` retains the exact host object `{claim_id,container_id,profile,origin,runner_pid,control_socket,next_sequence,staging_claim_id,staging_root,publisher_claim_id,publication_authorization_id}` from stage 5 through stage 8; container-resident `browser_session.py` alone retains the separate live `ClinicBrowserContexts`, and neither object crosses that process boundary; stage transitions cannot reconstruct it, and the restored web/network never appears in that object. It first consumes the owner password/start frame, POSTs `action=start`, and returns `pending-ready`; only then is helper 1 launched and its fresh pending-enrollment pipe sent through the owner code-confirm frame. Helpers 2, 3, and 4 are separate confirmed-next-counter processes for clinic-admin, receptionist, and physician `provision_staff` PTYs in that order; each waits from the newly committed `last_t` for a strictly later counter, exits before the next starts, and is consumed exactly once. After their users exist, clinic-admin and physician each send password/start, wait for `pending-ready`, then launch helpers 5 and 6 respectively and send their separate pending-enrollment pipes through code-confirm; receptionist has no TOTP device. Exactly seven secret frames run (four password-bearing starts/logins and three code-only confirms). No helper/FD is reused; no QR decoding, seed export, token argv/env/file/log, or owner/raw-RLS device query is allowed. Stage receipts expose only stage/timeout/start/end/exit/output hash. The bounded artifact protocol stages beneath the F3 orchestration filesystem claim, whose published-output list is empty. The controller sends the hash-only `publish-request`; the outer supervisor revalidates the complete runtime suite staging, durably publishes it through the persistent publisher UUID's exact `f3-artifacts` authorization under `final.lock`, and returns only a journal-backed `publish-result`. The controller then sends `artifact-published` to the still-live runner, receives its final suite acknowledgement, and sends `publish-ack`; stage success requires the supervisor's durable `controller-acknowledged` response. On the same success branch, the exclusive publisher later owns `f3-receipt` and `f3-manifest`; the F3 orchestration claim owns none of them, and both authorizations remain unpublished/empty on failure. The no-replace F3 manifest is exact canonical JSON `{schema_version:1,lane:"F3",sha,tree_sha,decision:"APPROVE",entries}` whose sorted entries are exact `{relative_path,sha256,media_kind}` objects for redacted receipt, exported screenshot manifest/screenshots, and summaries; it rejects symlinks, unlisted/extra files, and self-reference so F4 validates the lane transitively. Cleanup proves all claims, containers, networks, borrower attachments followed later by owner resources, listeners, journaled controller/stage/helper PIDs, CA/private leaves, the sole source-runner XDG NSS DB/profile and private control socket, six helper FDs/processes, dump, and credentials absent; the canonical ledger stays open.
  Final-E2E bounded stage contract: `final_e2e.sh` performs only the closed argv and input-frozen absolute launcher verification above, touches no claim or stage evidence, and immediately execs `final_e2e_supervisor.py` under the exact empty/allowlisted environment and verified absolute `.venv/bin/python -I -P -B` launcher; no uv, virtualenv discovery, PATH lookup, environment/config file, user site, bytecode, or cache action occurs. The supervisor validates frozen launcher/input identity, calls Linux `prctl(PR_SET_CHILD_SUBREAPER,1)` before any fork, creates-or-recovers its secret-free outer journal, and is the sole process-spawn owner. To start the absolute `final_e2e_controller.py`, a stage command, or a helper it forks a child whose minimal pre-exec code sets `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG=SIGKILL`, verifies the unchanged parent PID, creates its session/process group, signals ready, and blocks on a private barrier; the supervisor moves the child into the exact journal-bound cgroup-v2 kill domain, verifies membership, then records and fsyncs PID/PGID/start ticks plus cgroup identity before releasing that barrier. EOF or parent change exits before repository, claim, credential, or product action. The controller receives the same minimal environment plus only explicitly duplicated private secret/control FDs and input-frozen attempt ID, claim IDs, SHA/hash, orchestration-staging, and descendant-cgroup identity arguments; the ledger, stable lock, proof, authority root, failure-receipt root, and terminal control path remain supervisor-only; the terminal evidence path and persistent-publisher capability remain supervisor-only and are never included in child argv/environment/FDs; the controller can only submit/replay the exact hash-only `publish-request|publish-ack` RPC over this already-authenticated socket and receive `publish-result`; every open is nofollow and identity-checked, and only the supervisor may mutate the F3 journal or common failure receipt. The child controller owns every stage filesystem, Git SHA/tree/status/worktree, and browser assertion while requesting every ledger/claim transition through the authenticated supervisor RPC and implements two closed primitives. `run_in_process_stage(name,seconds,callable)` is mandatory for stages 5-8: it calls a controller method in the same main process, keeps the same nonserializable host `BrowserSupervisorSession` while the runner process keeps its live contexts, uses monotonic remaining-time accounting plus `signal.setitimer` and bounded socket/Playwright/PTY operation timeouts, converts expiry to typed `StageDeadline`, and never execs or serializes the stage/session. That callable may supervise bounded child-only management/helper processes without replacing the controller or session. `run_child_stage(name,seconds,argv)` is allowed only for stages 1-4 and 9-12; it sends one closed stage request over the private supervisor control socket. The supervisor rebuilds and allowlist-validates argv, barrier-journals and directly parents the child, enforces TERM->30-second wait->KILL plus `waitpid`, clears the active-child journal only after reaping, and returns a typed result. Every management/TOTP helper launched inside stages 5-8 uses the same supervisor RPC and barrier; the controller never forks or owns an unjournaled process group. The closed ordered stages/timeouts are (1) `preflight-and-claims` 120; (2) `tls-materializer-source-db` 600; (3) `owner-release-migrations` 900; (4) `owner-bootstrap` 300; (5) `source-https-and-runner-start` 300, which runs in process, intent-fsyncs/creates-or-adopts/inspects/prepares/starts/bootstrap-attests/activates the exact claimed source `container-https` runner, constructs the sole host `BrowserSupervisorSession`, requires the frozen runner available set `availability patient runtime-https scheduling`, and retains its private control socket while runner-resident `browser_session.py` retains the live contexts; (6) `owner-enrollment-start-ack-helper-confirm` 300 in the same process; (7) `three-owner-helpers-three-provisions-two-staff-enrollments` 900 in the same process, including four generated-password PTYs, admin/physician start-ack-helper-confirm, and receptionist password login; (8) `browser-visual` 1800 in the same process, using the unchanged stage-5 object/private socket to run `runtime-https` with invariant claim/container/profile/origin/runner PID and contiguous sequence; (9) `audit-posture` 600; (10) `restore-rehearsal` 1800; (11) `restored-https-smoke-audit` 600, which starts only the restored production web borrower, read-only verifies restored provisioning rows/ACL/audit posture, and performs trusted `curl --cacert`, readiness, database-role, RLS, and audit probes with no provisioning command, helper, password, TOTP, runner, session, or browser action; and (12) `reverse-cleanup` 300. Controller `HUP|INT|TERM` handling cancels the current in-process deadline or requests supervisor termination of the current journaled child group, then stops every recorded Docker/runner/helper claim, closes and zeroizes secret/control buffers, performs idempotent reverse-topological cleanup under the 300-second bound, validates resource/shared-evidence baseline, and emits bounded non-green redacted output; the child reports a typed terminal outcome and performs best-effort cleanup, but only the still-alive outer supervisor observes wait status, reaps every direct/adopted descendant available to the live subreaper, requires the complete kill-domain `cgroup.events` value `populated 0`, independently reconciles every journal/ledger identity and baseline, and afterward fsyncs/seals/re-hashes the outer journal at `failure-ready` before it no-replace writes the common F3 receipt referencing that immutable hash. A deterministic callable/child nonzero is `product-assertion`; typed timeout/signal/infrastructure and secondary cleanup causes follow fixed precedence. The external F3 command addresses the supervisor and gives it 360 seconds of TERM grace; the supervisor gives the child 300 seconds for reverse cleanup, then KILLs/reaps the direct child, invokes `cgroup.kill`, waits for `populated 0`, and completes independent recovery before returning. Contract tests hang every in-process operation and child stage, crash/SIGKILL the stage child, TERM the supervisor during stages 5-8, prove the host session object identity and runner PID survive normal 5->8 transitions without any host Playwright handle, and prove the surviving supervisor writes a receipt only after bounded cleanup/reap and baseline equality, while a next-entry non-parent recovery only authenticates, signals, and observes disappearance as specified below. A dedicated launcher test snapshots repository, `.venv`, lockfile, cache, evidence, and environment-visible sentinel state immediately before shell exec and at the supervisor's instrumented first instruction; it proves zero launcher mutation and poisons inherited `PATH`, every `UV_*`, `VIRTUAL_ENV`, `PYTHONPATH`, `PYTHONHOME`, user-site/config/env files, proxy variables, and alternate executable paths, requiring the exact frozen interpreter/script hashes and minimal environment to win. After the orchestration claim becomes active, the controller starts one secret-free heartbeat thread before any service or browser stage: every 20 seconds it sends an authenticated private-channel heartbeat RPC and never opens or writes the F3 journal. The supervisor takes the stable lock, refreshes the entire active dependency graph in topological order, atomically records the monotonic sequence and UTC completion as sole journal writer, and returns a typed acknowledgement; either side signals failure if any request, refresh, journal fsync, or acknowledgement is late, stale, or nonzero. Product/browser operations require the latest completion younger than 45 seconds; heartbeat failure cancels the current operation and enters cleanup. Reverse cleanup first blocks new operations, stops and joins the heartbeat within 10 seconds, then releases borrowers/owners. Fake-clock tests span at least 181 seconds, stall each refresh boundary, and prove no request proceeds past 60-second ledger freshness.
  Final-E2E outer-supervisor recovery contract: Todo 20 adds `ops/testing/f3_recovery.py` and `ops/testing/f3_kill_domain.py`; the supervisor is the sole writer of canonical `<attempt_root>/f3-supervisor.json`, mode 0600 while mutable and changed exactly once to mode 0400 at terminal success or sealed `failure-ready` before common failure-receipt publication, atomically replaced under the stable lock with exact keys `{schema_version,attempt_id,sha,creation_boot_id,recovery_boot_id,inputs_sha256,launcher_manifest_sha256,state,supervisor_pid,supervisor_start_ticks,cgroup_parent_path,cgroup_parent_device,cgroup_parent_inode,cgroup_path,cgroup_device,cgroup_inode,controller_pid,controller_pgid,controller_start_ticks,controller_barrier_released,active_child_kind,active_child_name,active_child_pid,active_child_pgid,active_child_start_ticks,active_child_barrier_released,heartbeat_sequence,heartbeat_at_utc,publication_records,started_at_utc,controller_ended_at_utc,termination_kind,boot_disappearance_sha256,wait_exit_code,wait_signal,timed_out,typed_outcome_sha256,kill_domain_removal_kind,kill_domain_removed_at_utc,cleanup_verified,updated_at_utc}`. `state` is exactly `prepared|kill-domain-intent|kill-domain-ready|controller-barrier|controller-running|controller-terminal|recovering|kill-domain-remove-intent|kill-domain-removed|success|failure-ready`. `termination_kind` is null before a terminal observation, `wait-result` for a normal child/controller outcome, and `boot-disappearance` only when stale-boot recovery authenticates different creation/current boots; `boot_disappearance_sha256` is null except in that last case, where it hashes the exact prior/current boot observations and wait exit/signal/typed-outcome fields are null with `timed_out=false`. In `prepared`, `creation_boot_id` is the current boot, `recovery_boot_id=null`, the input-frozen cgroup parent path/device/inode are nonnull and revalidated, child-cgroup/controller/child/heartbeat/outcome fields are null, and both removal fields are null; `kill-domain-intent` requires the exact absent child path derived from attempt ID with null child device/inode and no child process; `kill-domain-ready` requires the nofollow-validated owned child-cgroup path/device/inode and no child process; `controller-barrier` requires the complete controller PID/PGID/start tuple with `controller_barrier_released=false`; `controller-running` requires that same tuple with `controller_barrier_released=true`; the active-child fields are all null or one complete `stage|helper` identity and its barrier flag; `publication_records` is an append-only authorization-ordered array of the exact RPC/internal record shape, empty before a request and thereafter obeying only `requested -> imported -> published -> controller-acknowledged` for `f3-artifacts`; supervisor-internal `f3-receipt|f3-manifest` records may follow `requested -> imported -> published` only on the success branch after suite/controller success is durable. On failure, any durable predecessor-valid `f3-artifacts` prefix remains immutable while `f3-receipt|f3-manifest` records and destinations are absent/unpublished, with identities/hashes preserved; `controller-terminal|recovering` preserve every last nonnull identity and publication record until the domain is empty. `kill-domain-remove-intent` is locked/fsynced before any `rmdir` and requires all recorded members absent, exact `populated 0` when the child exists, `cleanup_verified=false`, null removal fields, and either the captured same-boot child identity or a null identity only when no child was ever created; on a changed boot it instead requires `recovery_boot_id` equal the freshly read current boot and the exact old path absent. `kill-domain-removed` preserves the prior path/device/inode as a tombstone, requires the path absent, sets `kill_domain_removal_kind=rmdir|boot-disappearance` and nonnull `kill_domain_removed_at_utc`, and still has `cleanup_verified=false`. `success|failure-ready` may be reached only from `kill-domain-removed` after baseline/resource proof sets `cleanup_verified=true`; the removal kind/timestamp and every prior identity remain immutable. No secret, URL, credential, browser token, raw output, terminal destination, or ambient evidence path enters the journal; publication records carry only authorization/claim identities and hashes. `creation_boot_id` never changes; `recovery_boot_id` is null on same-boot cleanup and becomes the one current boot ID only during changed-boot recovery. The first mutation is locked/fsynced `prepared` before any child, claim, secret, or resource. After revalidating cgroup v2 plus the frozen parent identity, readable `cgroup.events`, and writable `cgroup.procs|cgroup.kill`, the supervisor derives exact absent child path `<cgroup-parent>/clinic-os-phase1a-<attempt-id>-f3` and locked/fsyncs `kill-domain-intent` before `mkdir`. It then creates only that child, nofollow-validates its canonical path/device/inode and executor ownership, requires empty `cgroup.procs`, no descendant cgroup, and `populated 0`, and only then journals `kill-domain-ready`. Recovery at `kill-domain-intent` may create an absent child or adopt an existing child only when the frozen parent and exact basename match and the child is nofollow-regular-as-cgroup, executor-owned, empty, unpopulated, and contains no descendants; it then either journals `kill-domain-ready` before use or fsyncs `kill-domain-remove-intent` before removing that empty child. Any direct unjournaled removal, member, foreign owner, symlink, identity ambiguity, or extra child blocks without signal or deletion. Every controller/stage/helper child sets `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG=SIGKILL`, verifies parent PID, creates its session/process group, signals ready, and blocks; before release the supervisor moves that PID into the journaled cgroup, verifies `cgroup.procs`, reads start ticks, writes/fsyncs the controller-specific or active-child identity with barrier false, releases the barrier, then writes/fsyncs true. Descendants inherit the cgroup even when a stage invokes `make`, shell, Docker CLI, PostgreSQL tools, or another executable. Barrier EOF, supervisor change, cgroup-membership failure, or journal failure exits before application code. The controller heartbeat never writes the journal: its authenticated private RPC asks the supervisor to refresh the full dependency DAG; the supervisor takes the stable lock, performs the refresh, writes heartbeat sequence/time, and acknowledges. A live supervisor remains direct parent/subreaper, directly reaps available descendants, handles `HUP|INT|TERM` by TERM/KILL plus `waitpid`, then invokes `cgroup.kill` if needed and requires exact `cgroup.events populated 0`. It preserves every identity, locked/fsyncs `kill-domain-remove-intent`, performs `rmdir` only against the same nofollow-validated child inode, locked/fsyncs `kill-domain-removed` with `kill_domain_removal_kind=rmdir`, and only then proves zero claims/resources/private FDs/profiles/tmpfs, restores shared/resource baselines, sets `cleanup_verified=true`, and either reaches success or fsyncs/seals/re-hashes `failure-ready` before it writes the one common F3 receipt that binds those immutable journal bytes. If the supervisor is SIGKILLed, the next `final_e2e.sh`, any `final_wave_controller.py` form, or `isolation_ledger.py reject` first runs `f3_recovery.py` under the stable lock. At `kill-domain-intent`, a successor follows only the empty-domain adoption/removal rule above and never signals an unjournaled member. At `kill-domain-ready` through `recovering`, a same-boot successor is not a parent and never claims non-parent `waitpid`; it rejects a live matching supervisor, validates the frozen cgroup parent and exact child-cgroup path/device/inode, writes `1` to `cgroup.kill`, waits boundedly for exact `populated 0`, validates every recorded PID/start tuple absent, locked/fsyncs `kill-domain-remove-intent`, removes only that empty inode, then locked/fsyncs `kill-domain-removed`. A successor at `kill-domain-remove-intent` accepts an absent path only as the idempotent crash-after-`rmdir` case, otherwise revalidates the same empty inode before `rmdir`; at `kill-domain-removed` it performs no cgroup mutation. It restores baselines and creates-or-validates the one F3 receipt only after the durable removed state. PID/PGID observations are defense-in-depth only; successor cleanup never relies on a possibly reused PGID. On the creation boot, an absent child is expected only in `prepared`, before `mkdir` in `kill-domain-intent`, or after the fsynced `kill-domain-remove-intent`; absence from `kill-domain-ready` through `recovering` before removal intent blocks. A boot change is the sole additional absence case: under the stable lock recovery records the fresh `recovery_boot_id`, proves the old Linux cgroup/process domain cannot survive that boot boundary, requires every recorded PID/start tuple and the exact old child path absent, revalidates a fresh current cgroup-v2 observation without treating the new parent inode as the old authority, and advances through `kill-domain-remove-intent` to `kill-domain-removed` with `kill_domain_removal_kind=boot-disappearance` and no signal, kill, or `rmdir`; the outer stale-boot ledger journal still owns all surviving Docker/filesystem cleanup. On a changed boot, `reconcile --stale-boot` is the sole caller allowed to drive this sequence. Its outer prepared state binds the existing F3 journal path and initial hash. During physical cleaning it first advances F3 durably through `kill-domain-removed`, then the outer action plan removes every remaining Docker/filesystem resource. Only after the journaled claims-prune transition has removed all nonpublisher claims may `receipts-finalizing` run the F3 finalizer: it proves restored baselines, sets `cleanup_verified=true`, sets `termination_kind=boot-disappearance` plus the authenticated boot hash and null wait/outcome fields, fsyncs/seals/re-hashes the supervisor journal at `failure-ready`, binds `f3_final_journal_sha256`, then creates-or-byte-validates the canonical failure receipt referencing that final hash and binds `f3_receipt_path|f3_receipt_sha256`. These immutable artifacts must exist before publisher release intent or the atomic ledger boot update; the supervisor journal never stores or follows the receipt hash. `final_e2e`, all final-wave forms, `reject`, `close`, and archive remain blocked until the outer recovery reaches complete after receipt finalization, optional publisher release, and exact post-update ledger validation. Any present replaced/foreign child, same-boot unexplained absence, populated domain that cannot be killed, unjournaled matching task resource, unverifiable identity, or baseline mismatch blocks F4/rejection/archive. ## Commit strategy

- Exactly one primary Conventional Commit per todo, integrated in dependency order (not necessarily numeric order: Todos 17-18 precede UI Todos 14-16). Every primary commit is independently buildable at its declared boundary and has the exact subject listed in that todo.
- Every exact subject below is an intent line explaining why. Each commit follows the workspace Lore protocol with contiguous Git-native `Confidence`, `Scope-risk`, and `Tested` trailers plus `Constraint`, meaningful `Rejected`, `Directive`, and `Not-tested` only where they add real decision context; empty boilerplate trailers are forbidden.
- Do not squash the schema/RLS, audit, concurrency, production-settings, image, or recovery commits. No merge commit unless an unavoidable shared-file conflict is documented and independently verified.
- If any F1-F3 lane or F4 scope precheck rejects/fails, skip pre-F4/F4/final; if pre-F4 fails, skip F4/final; if F4 semantically rejects, retain its publisher-canonical rejecting `F4-final.txt` plus lane-F4 receipt but never create `final.json`; if the gate fails before F4 publication, retain only the ORCH-final-gate receipt and neither artifact; if canonical APPROVE publishes but publisher/freezer completion fails, retain approving F4 plus the ORCH-final-gate receipt and no `final.json`. In every such F1-F4/ORCH case, stop/teardown every final-lane resource, release all claims, verify resource plus namespace/shared-evidence baselines, and use only the originating common receipt(s) without manufacturing downstream ORCH causes. Initialize exact `USER_CONTEXT_ARGV=()` and `USER_MUTATION_ARGV=()` for every failure-receipt rejection. If the user requests an in-scope repair after sealed approvals but before success close, instead first run exact `TERMINAL_REVALIDATION="$(timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=10s 120s uv run --frozen --no-sync --no-env-file python ops/testing/isolation_ledger.py reconcile --terminal-final "$(pwd -P)/.omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final/final.json" --sha "$SHA")"`, require its one authenticated absolute path, then run exact `USER_AUTHORIZATION="$(timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=10s 120s uv run --frozen --no-sync --no-env-file python ops/testing/isolation_ledger.py authorize-user-fix --sha "$SHA" --terminal-revalidation "$TERMINAL_REVALIDATION" --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final)"`, require its one authenticated absolute current-boot mode-0400 binding path, and set exact `USER_CONTEXT_ARGV=(--user-requested-fix --user-authorization "$USER_AUTHORIZATION")` plus `USER_MUTATION_ARGV=(--user-authorization "$USER_AUTHORIZATION")`. The first invocation records the sole boot-stable user intent; same-boot stdout-loss retry reuses the exact binding, and after a reboot the same intent authorizes appending the fresh binding without a second user request. On both boots, terminal reconciliation has already completed and persisted the sole final refresh before the USER binding; no ordinary `verify --refresh` or other ledger mutation is permitted afterward. Every USER context/reject/close command independently requires the ledger to remain at the terminal artifact’s exact `post_update_ledger_sha256`; the changed-boot artifact remains the proof consumed by rejection close. The rejection/archive/restart sequence runs under environment-closed Bash with `set -euo pipefail` and begins exactly `mapfile -d '' -t REJECTION_CONTEXT < <(timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=10s 120s uv run --frozen --no-sync --no-env-file python ops/testing/isolation_ledger.py rejection-context "${USER_CONTEXT_ARGV[@]}" --sha "$SHA" --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final --format nul)` followed by `test "${#REJECTION_CONTEXT[@]}" -ge 10`; then run exact `ATTEMPT_ID="${REJECTION_CONTEXT[0]}"; INPUTS_SHA_OR_NONE="${REJECTION_CONTEXT[1]}"; PRE_F4_SHA_OR_NONE="${REJECTION_CONTEXT[2]}"; FINAL_SHA_OR_NONE="${REJECTION_CONTEXT[3]}"; AUTHORITY_WORKSPACE="${REJECTION_CONTEXT[4]}"; AUTHORITY_ROOT="${REJECTION_CONTEXT[5]}"; APPROVED_PLAN="${REJECTION_CONTEXT[6]}"; FEATURE_WORKTREE="${REJECTION_CONTEXT[7]}"; TERMINAL_REVALIDATION_OR_NONE="${REJECTION_CONTEXT[8]}"`; require it is exactly `none` for ordinary rejection and for USER only when the supplied current binding plus terminal artifact say creation boot/`boot_changed=false`, or that binding's authenticated absolute current terminal path otherwise; then exact `REASON_ARGV=(); for ((i=9; i<${#REJECTION_CONTEXT[@]}; i++)); do REASON_ARGV+=(--reason "${REJECTION_CONTEXT[$i]}"); done`, and exact `CLOSE_BOOT_ARGV=(); if [[ "$TERMINAL_REVALIDATION_OR_NONE" != none ]]; then CLOSE_BOOT_ARGV=(--terminal-revalidation "$TERMINAL_REVALIDATION_OR_NONE"); fi`. While the canonical ledger is open, run exact `REJECTION_SPEC_SHA="$(timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=10s 120s uv run --frozen --no-sync --no-env-file python ops/testing/isolation_ledger.py reject "${USER_MUTATION_ARGV[@]}" --sha "$SHA" "${REASON_ARGV[@]}" --inputs-sha256 "$INPUTS_SHA_OR_NONE" --pre-f4-sha256 "$PRE_F4_SHA_OR_NONE" --final-sha256 "$FINAL_SHA_OR_NONE")"`; require one lowercase 64-hex line. Then run exact `timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=10s 120s uv run --frozen --no-sync --no-env-file python ops/testing/isolation_ledger.py close "${USER_MUTATION_ARGV[@]}" "${CLOSE_BOOT_ARGV[@]}"`, followed by exact `timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=10s 120s uv run --frozen --no-sync --no-env-file python ops/testing/isolation_ledger.py archive-rollover --closed-attempt "$ATTEMPT_ID" --rejected-sha "$SHA" --control-root .omo/evidence/clinic-os-phase1a-final --rejection-spec-sha256 "$REJECTION_SPEC_SHA"`. Through the durable source-versus-bundled locator state machine above, archive no-replace stores the validated absent-or-partial-or-complete control tree, complete attempt root, and closed ledger, then writes the exact rejection bundle; never edit it. After bundle validation, run exact `EXECUTION_HOST_PREFLIGHT="$(timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=10s 120s uv run --frozen --no-sync --no-env-file python ops/testing/execution_host_preflight.py publish --authority-root "$AUTHORITY_ROOT")"`, require one absolute current-boot proof path, then run exact `timeout --signal=TERM --kill-after=10s 120s uv run --frozen --no-sync --no-env-file python ops/testing/isolation_ledger.py snapshot --after-archive "$ATTEMPT_ID" --approved-plan "$APPROVED_PLAN" --authority-workspace "$AUTHORITY_WORKSPACE" --authority-root "$AUTHORITY_ROOT" --foundation-sha cffbb1900ae2132560f20c27fcf1a514a1ef71aa --worktree "$FEATURE_WORKTREE" --execution-host-preflight "$EXECUTION_HOST_PREFLIGHT"`. The context command and each mutating command independently nofollow-revalidate every value, so array substitution cannot widen authority. If any USER command loses stdout or the boot changes while the ledger remains open, restart at terminal reconciliation and `authorize-user-fix`; the existing exact intent selects recovery, a fresh current binding is appended, `rejection-context` is rerun, and an existing USER rejection spec is byte-validated unchanged. If the ledger is already closed, validate its stored `rejection_close` and continue archive rollover without creating a current-boot binding. If `retry_kind=evidence-only`, keep the exact SHA/tree, create no commit or supplemental receipt, keep `next_fix_sequence`, and rerun all F1-F4. If `retry_kind=source-fix`, return to the owning todo and add—never amend—a narrow Lore review-fix commit with `Owning-todo`, `Review-lane`, and `Rejected-SHA`; create its globally next-sequenced machine-bound receipt, then rerun focused QA and all F1-F4 at the new SHA. Final freeze accepts commit coverage through primary/fix lineage while permitting zero-commit evidence-only attempts. Only newest common-SHA receipts approve. No completed bundle is excluded, reopened, overwritten, moved, or deleted.
- Before each commit: focused tests green, `uv run ruff check <changed Python>` and `uv run ruff format --check <changed Python>` green, strict mypy green for changed modules, `git diff --check` green, no secret/PII in diff/evidence, and only task-owned paths staged.
- Preserve the exact foundation commit/tag/branch and all existing migration blobs. The feature branch begins at `cffbb1900ae2132560f20c27fcf1a514a1ef71aa`; no rebase or history rewrite after final verification starts.
- Do not push, open a PR, or create a release tag in this plan. After F1-F4 all approve, surface the exact SHA/tree/evidence with the zero-claim ledger still open; an in-scope requested fix uses the USER rejection path, while explicit acceptance authorizes only the refreshed terminal close and final receipt.

## Success criteria
